### Session 28r (same day) - RV map decoded + net engine has a first-class LAN mode (direction B needs NO PRUDP)

Ghidra import+analysis of the running `FC3_d3d11.dll` copy (md5 46bcc097, project `FC3d3d11_live`, ~48 min),
then decompiled the 28q chains (`rv_decompile.txt`). Maps:

- **RendezVous bootstrap chain (top->down):** game entry `FUN_1044123b` -> `FUN_1127a3b0` -> `FUN_1127a300`
  -> **`FUN_11279ba0` (the gate: returns 1 only if the RV net-op `FUN_112492a0` succeeds, else 0)** ->
  on success `FUN_1127a1d0` does the post-RV session op. Config side: `FUN_1122f870` parses a server URL string,
  `FUN_1122f680` splits `address=<host>;port=<n>`, resolver `FUN_11211290` = `inet_addr` else `gethostbyname`
  (IPv4 stored at obj+4; special-case "255.255.255.255"). Lazy "ready" flag getter `FUN_11233110` returns obj+0x4a.
- **Net engine init `FUN_1092d256` (caller only via a vtable at 0x115c0e24):** has a FIRST-CLASS connection mode:
  `param_1[0xe]` = 0 -> logs **"LAN"**, 1 -> **"Online"**, 2 -> **"Offline"**; plus a singleton
  `(**(*DAT_11e42a90 + 4))()` returning 1/2/3 mapped to 3/2/1 (a network-type selector). It binds the echo UDP
  socket (`FUN_1041a75a` -> `FUN_10e5bdc0`) and only errors "Net engine initialization failed.(UDP binding)"
  (code -0xf4229) on a bind failure. `FUN_105516a8` is the per-frame session pump (states 0..4 over a peer list).

**Direction-B conclusion (de-risked):** the game ALREADY has a LAN netcode path; co-op only fails because the
session is put in "Online" mode and then RendezVous refuses the FC3 tenant. The lever is the **network-mode
selector**, not PRUDP. Concrete next steps:
1. Find every writer of the mode (fields around `obj+0xe`, the `DAT_11e42a90` singleton + its vtable slot +4) and
   where the co-op menu sets Online vs LAN.
2. Frida **in-memory** experiment (no disk writes): force the net mode to LAN (and/or force `FUN_11279ba0` to
   return 1) at the co-op connect, and observe whether "service unavailable" disappears and which sockets/peers the
   LAN path then uses. This is the safe go/no-go test before any on-disk patch.

> **28r addendum (rv2 pass):** gate FUN_11279ba0 first reads global online flag `FUN_11245090`=`DAT_11f07e48`
> (0 -> immediate fail); net-op FUN_112492a0 reads config **"SandboxUrl"**, status 2/3 -> fail, else needs
> `FUN_11233110` (address resolved) -> success; post-RV FUN_1127a1d0 builds a **UbiAuthentication** session
> (RendezVous/Services/UbiAuthentication/.../UbiAuthenticationClient.cpp). Forcing the gate alone dead-ends at
> UbiAuth, so direction B must use the engine's LAN mode (`FUN_1092d256` param+0xe:0), not fake online success.
> Network-type singleton DAT_11e42a90 is read ~80x / written once (constructor FUN_100bc087).


### Session 28s (same day) - co-op session dispatcher decodes a NON-RV path (direction B lever) + live Frida now blocked by anti-injection

Fast re-decompiles of the analyzed `FC3d3d11_live` (no re-analysis) resolved the mode decision:

- **Co-op session-create dispatcher `FUN_10441fb6`** (invoked via a vtable at 0x11526798): resolves a per-session
  record (`FUN_10431a1b` over `FUN_1041dba9`; record stride 0x90, id at +0x10) and branches on a byte flag `*(rec+5)`:
  - flag `== 0` -> **`FUN_1044123b` = the ONLINE path** -> RV bootstrap (`FUN_1127a380`/`FUN_1127a3b0`) -> UbiAuth (dead).
  - flag `!= 0` -> **`FUN_10443663` = the NON-RV path** -> `FUN_11275f40(rec, &id, ..., this+1)` creates/joins the
    session WITHOUT RendezVous. Both report failures through the error-code dispatcher `FUN_104532ec`.

So the engine already contains a non-RV co-op session path; direction B = make the co-op session take
`FUN_10443663`/`FUN_11275f40` instead of the online `FUN_1044123b`. Open questions (next static work): confirm
`FUN_11275f40` is a direct storm::echo/UDP peer host (no Ubisoft), and locate where the record +5 LAN flag is set
(i.e. whether the co-op host/join UI can create a LAN-type session, or it is hard-wired online).

**Live Frida is now blocked:** the x86 injector is healthy (attaching Frida to a 32-bit SysWOW64 process succeeds in
2.1s, same frida-helper-x86 path the game uses), but attach to `farcry3_d3d11.exe` reliably times out even on a fresh
game after purging all frida-helpers. => FC3's anti-injection is now consistently refusing attach (in 28q the same
tracer attached fine). The interactive force-probe is therefore unreliable; continue via static + on-disk patch tests.


### Session 28t (same day) - DEFINITIVE: campaign co-op session is RendezVous-mandated (no in-engine LAN path)

Final fast decompiles settle direction B's feasibility:

- The "other" co-op flavor `FUN_10443663 -> FUN_11275f40` STILL calls the RendezVous gate `FUN_11279ba0`, then
  **`FUN_11275d90`**, which references `.../online/RendezVous/RendezVous/Services/SubAccount/src/Client/SubAccountClient.cpp`
  and calls `FUN_112536a0(...)` = create a **RendezVous Session**. So this branch is the RendezVous **host** path
  (SubAccount + RVSession). The `+5==0` branch (`FUN_1127a1d0`) is the RendezVous **UbiAuthentication join** path.
- Therefore BOTH campaign co-op session flavors go through RendezVous (`FUN_11279ba0` is mandatory), which needs
  OnlineConfigService/`SandboxUrl` + Ubi auth - all dead. The net engine's LAN/Online/Offline (`FUN_1092d256`) is
  only transport initialization; there is **no non-RendezVous co-op session code path** to force.

**Reassessment:** direction B as "force LAN / skip RendezVous in-engine" is NOT viable - nothing to redirect to.
The only remaining technical routes are large:
  - **(A) emulate the Ubisoft online stack** the co-op path needs (OnlineConfigService SOAP -> SandboxUrl,
    RendezVous/PRUDP session server, UbiAuthentication, SubAccount) so the client completes its own handshake.
  - **(A') deeper patch at the RendezVous Session layer**: intercept RV session peer-address exchange (PRUDP) so
    the game is handed peer LAN addresses instead of relays; still requires understanding RV/PRUDP session semantics.
Reusable assets so far: the loader-proxy (orbit fakes, ABI-correct) and the full RV call map (28r-28t). Recommend
documenting this blocker with the map + the PR, then deciding whether to fund an (A)/(A') spike.


### Session 28u (same day) - A' spike verdict: peer-address injection needs full RV session emulation; both A and A' are large

A'-spike (static, no edits) traced the RV host session create `FUN_112536a0` (`RendezVous/Core/.../BackEndServices.cpp`):
it requires `FUN_11237670` (RV connected), builds the session via factory `FUN_112532a0(...)` stored at `obj+0x74`,
starts it via a vtable call (`*vtable+4`) and `FUN_112283a0(obj,0)`. The RV Session client is exactly the component
that, over PRUDP, performs matchmaking and hands each peer the other's address. Therefore:

- **A' (patch the RV-session layer to inject LAN peer addresses) is not cheaper than A** - to form a session the game
  still needs the RV session to deliver real peer addresses and drive the storm::echo exchange; without the RV service
  (or a reimplementation of its session semantics) there are no addresses to hand out, so the session cannot form.
- **A (emulate the Ubisoft online stack)** requires, at minimum: (1) `OnlineConfigService` SOAP returning a
  `SandboxUrl`/rendezvous config, (2) a **RendezVous server speaking PRUDP** (RC4/DH + ticketing + mutex/state machine
  + session matchmaking), (3) `UbiAuthentication` login, (4) `SubAccount` - all for the FC3 title. Weeks-scale.

**Bottom line (honest):** FC3 campaign co-op is deeply coupled to Ubisoft's RendezVous + UbiAuth + SubAccount stack;
there is no in-engine LAN path and no cheap gate-patch. Reusable, shippable assets: the ABI-correct loader proxy
(orbit fakes) and the complete static RV call map (28r-28u). Recommendation: ship those (PR) and record the RV wall;
pursuing A is a multi-protocol project that should be scoped as its own effort, not continued piecemeal here.


### Session 28v (same day) - reconciled with upstream NOTES.md: storm::echo LAN/loopback shim is the tractable path (soften 28u)

Read the upstream `Bantila/FarCry3Coop/NOTES.md`. It maps more than 28u assumed, so correct the verdict:

- **`storm::echo` is a built-in LAN + loopback matchmaking layer**: `storm::echo::lan::inEvent::DiscoverSession`,
  `lan::outEvent::DiscoveredSession`, `lan::inFunc::GenerateSessionId`, `lan::outEvent::BroadcastLanDiscovery2`;
  tunables `LanMatchmakingReuseAddress`, `LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable` (multi-instance on one PC),
  `LanMaxUploadInbps`, `SessionJoinTimeoutInMs`, an "LAN Match Making Port" option, and CLI launch `-coop`,
  `-client -ip <addr>` (host binds UDP 9000, client 9001), `-dedicated`, `-offline`, `-sessionuid`, `-password`.
  Gameplay bytes are plain UDP P2P; only session SETUP is service-bound. So offline 2-4 humans is a
  **session-establishment shim over storm::echo**, NOT a netcode rehost and NOT necessarily full Ubisoft emulation.
  (28u's "A is weeks-scale, no cheap path" is too strong - retracted to: the LAN/loopback shim is the route.)
- Two open blockers (upstream): the **SessionTicket** and **UDP crypto / session-key minting** (service vs host).
- Upstream's own 1.06 wall matches mine: a co-op session is not offline-constructible by field poking; the
  **1.05 mod route is the leading offline path** (mod FC3.dll `patch_solo_offline_coop_vanilla-262`; "Offline coop"
  world loads single-player via mod 262; 1.05 engine boots 1.06 data on the island).

**My contribution to upstream's open gate** ("does CampaignCoop host/join use storm::echo LAN discover or only the
online lobby?"): on the 1.06 DX11 module the co-op session dispatcher `FUN_10441fb6` -> `FUN_1044123b` (online) /
`FUN_10443663` (host) ALWAYS passes the RendezVous gate `FUN_11279ba0` -> RV `BackEndServices` session (`FUN_112536a0`);
there is **no storm::echo-LAN branch in the menu-driven co-op path**. So menu co-op is online-gated; a LAN shim must be
driven through the Storm-session/CLI entry (which exists but "offline flow never drives it") or by replacing the
`CSessionLoginOperationProvider_RdV` login provider with a local one.

**Alignment going forward:** work the shim on the 1.05 leading route (upstream), with FC3_106 addresses cross-mapped
via `fc3_addrmap.py`; the RDV provider replacement (A1) and storm::echo LAN discovery are the concrete levers.


### Session 28w - storm::echo LAN + SessionTicket classes confirmed present in the running module (DecompileLAN pass)

Fast Ghidra pass (`DecompileLAN.java`) over the analyzed `FC3d3d11_live` (running module md5 46bcc097): scanned
engine strings and decompiled their referrers. All target classes are **compiled-in and registered** (not stripped):

- **storm::echo LAN discovery events** (serialized message types): `lan::inEvent::DiscoverSession`
  (registrar `FUN_10e5eff0`), `lan::inEvent::SetDiscoveryHandler` (`FUN_10e5efc0`), base `lan::inEvent::Event`
  (`FUN_10e5ef90`), `lan::inEvent::internal*` (`FUN_10e75fa0`). LAN discovery is live in this build.
- **SessionTicket family** (the open blocker): `storm::echo::SessionTicket` (`FUN_10e755c0`, `FUN_10edf950`),
  `SessionTicketRequest` (`FUN_10e72b50`, `FUN_10edf880`), `SessionTicketAttach(ment)` (`FUN_10e72bb0`, `FUN_10edf7b0`)
  - all registered via `FactoryEntry` for network message (de)serialization. So the ticket is an exchange message; the
  mint/validate logic is in those classes' (de)serialize/attach methods (next place to look), not in the factory stub.
- **Join/create parameter schema** `FUN_1061e581` registers session op fields as data: `SearchIdAddress`, `JoinGame`,
  `JoinSessionId`, `SessionRefId`, `SessionType`, **`NetEngineType` (id 0x3c)**, `MatchMakingEnabled`, `PlaylistId`,
  `AccountType`, `SearchSlots`, ... => LAN vs Online (`NetEngineType`) and the peer `SearchIdAddress` are **settable session
  parameters**, i.e. the shim lever is data, not code-poking.
- **echo tunable registry** `FUN_107a227d` registers `LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable`, `LanMatchmakingReuseAddress`,
  `SessionTicketTimeoutInMs`, connection timeouts, etc. => LAN/loopback is **config-gated** (answers upstream's open question:
  yes, LanMatchmaking* are writable runtime tunables).
- **CFCX session ops** are registered factories: `CFCXCreateSessionOperation` (`FUN_107de8fa`), `CFCXJoinSessionOperation`
  (`FUN_1092e764`), `CFCXCreateSessionOpCtn` (`FUN_107de926`); `CSessionSearchIPOperation` referrer `FUN_10425918`.
- **A1 note:** the verbatim symbol `CSessionLoginOperationProvider_RdV` was NOT found by this scan in 46bcc097
  (upstream's provider vtable/methods are for a different module). In this build the RdV path is reached via the gate
  `FUN_11279ba0` chain (28t); the cleaner lever here is the join-param `NetEngineType` + storm::echo LAN events, not patching
  upstream's provider vtable.

**Verdict:** storm::echo LAN / join-param shim (route B/A1) is viable in the running module at the map level. Remaining
unknown to drill next: which handler mints/fills `SessionTicket` (the service-key vs host-key question upstream flagged).


### Session 28x - DECISIVE: the SessionTicket is SERVICE-minted (RendezVous JobTicketManager), not host-minted

Strings probe on the running module `FC3_d3d11.dll` (46bcc097). The SessionTicket lives entirely on the RendezVous auth side:

- `D:/Perforce/FC3/fc3/main/external/technology-group/online/RendezVous/RendezVous/Core/src/Client/Authentication/JobTicket`,
  `.../TicketManager`; jobs `JobTicketManagerLogin::{InitiateLogin,DeriveKey,ValidateKey,ProcessLoginResult}` (VA strings:
  DeriveKey `0x116c1a1c`, ValidateKey `0x116c1acc`), `JobTicketManagerAcquireTicket::{PrepareCall `0x116c123c`,
  ProcessResponse `0x116c17a8`}` (a request->response job to a service), plus `TicketGrantingProtocol` (`0x116c70f0`),
  `TICKET_PENDING`, `UseTicketRequestAttachment` (`0x11561550`), `RequestTicket`, `Invalid ticket.` (`0x11665184`).
- Game session keys come from RendezVous too: `E_SESSION_ERROR_RENDEZVOUS_GAMESESSION_INVALIDSESSIONKEY` (`0x116ecb7c`),
  `GameSessionKey`/`gameSessionKey`/`m_sessionKey`, and account/gator errors `E_SESSION_ERROR_ACCOUNT_COULD_NOT_RETRIEVE_NP_TICKET`
  (NP = NetPlay ticket), `E_SESSION_ERROR_TICKET_REQUEST_FAILED`, `E_SESSION_ERROR_GATOR_INVALID_SESSION_TICKET`.

**Verdict (answers the open host-vs-service question): the SessionTicket / GameSessionKey is minted by the SERVICE (RendezVous
JobTicketManager AcquireTicket + TicketGrantingProtocol, key via DeriveKey/ValidateKey). It is NOT host-minted.**

Consequences for the plan:
- Route "set NetEngineType=LAN and join by IP" is NOT sufficient **if the LAN/IP join still demands a valid SessionTicket**.
  Then offline co-op needs either (A) a service answering `AcquireTicket`/login (full-ish emulation), OR
  (B) a narrow CLIENT patch at the ticket path: make `JobTicketManagerAcquireTicket::ProcessResponse` /
  `JobTicketManagerLogin::ValidateKey` accept a locally-supplied ticket and inject a `GameSessionKey`, so no RV service is
  contacted. (B) is now the **cheapest viable lever** and its exact anchor points are the string VAs above (xref them in Ghidra).
- Still open (needs the join-flow decompile or a dynamic run, Frida blocked): whether the `storm::echo` LAN/loopback join
  (`lan::inEvent::DiscoverSession`) is EXEMPT from the RV JobTicket (classic LAN trust). If exempt, no ticket work is needed
  for same-LAN/loopback co-op. The decisive experiment: launch `-coop` host + `-client -ip` with
  `LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable=1` and observe whether a ticket request is issued / join succeeds.
- Concrete targets to decompile next (via `getReferencesTo` on the string VAs): the JobTicketManagerLogin / AcquireTicket
  methods (patch candidates for (B)) and the CFCX/LAN join flow (to test exemption).


### Session 28y - ticket patch-surface (B) mapped: localized, and the key only needs to MATCH between peers

Ghidra `DecompileTicket`/`Decompile1` pass on `FC3d3d11_live` (46bcc097). The ticket flow (real code, not registrars):

- **PrepareCall** `FUN_1124e5e0`: builds the AcquireTicket job, stores params (`param_1[0x1b..0x1e]`), sets the response
  handler to `FUN_1124d660`, name "JobTicketManagerAcquireTicket::PrepareCall", then `FUN_1121c8f0(&handler)` sends it.
- **ProcessResponse** `FUN_1124d660`: on the success branch writes the received ticket into an out buffer via
  `**(param_1+0x78) = *(local_c+0x10)`, calls vtable+4, and `FUN_1124a430(2)` (status=success). => **the ticket-inject point**.
- **ValidateKey** `FUN_1124d7b0`: gated by global `DAT_1174d35a`. Branch A (`==0`) calls **`FUN_112547c0`** and fails with
  `0x80010001`/status 3 if it returns 0. Branch B (`!=0`) calls `FUN_112547f0` (an alternate path).
- **`FUN_112547c0` / `FUN_112547f0` are thin VIRTUAL THUNKS** that dispatch the real crypto/derive through the vtable backend
  `PTR_PTR_117531f4` (slots +4/+8) with selector `DAT_11f07f1c`. So the ticket/key crypto is a **pluggable backend object**,
  not inline crypto.
- **DeriveKey** `FUN_1124e670`: checks job state `+0x114`, calls `FUN_11210ad0(...)`, and chains to ValidateKey
  (`FUN_1124d7b0`); key bytes carried at `+0x13c/+0x140`.
- Session error enum `FUN_113abb34`: `E_SESSION_ERROR_TICKET_REQUEST_FAILED`=5, `JOIN_FAILED*`, `SEARCH_FAILED*`, etc.
  (the observable failure surface).

**Key realization for a friends-only co-op mod:** the ticket's crypto only has to MATCH between the peers (both sides must
derive the same `GameSessionKey`), it does NOT have to be authentic to Ubisoft. So patch (B) is: (1) stop `PrepareCall` from
networking and fill the out-ticket buffer locally (from session id / a shared password), (2) make `ValidateKey`/`DeriveKey`
accept it (neutralize the `FUN_112547c0` crypto check or swap the `PTR_PTR_117531f4` backend for a deterministic local one).
Every co-op player installs the same mod, so the locally-derived key matches on all peers -> session forms with no RV service.
Surface is small (4 functions + 1 vtable backend), all in the running module. Next drill: dump the `PTR_PTR_117531f4` backend
vtable to pick the exact swap or the single validate-check to neutralize.


### Session 28z - FINALIZED patch (B): the ticket "crypto" is MD5 only; single validate slot identified

Ghidra `DecompileBackend`/`Decompile2` on `FC3d3d11_live` (46bcc097). The backend reached from `ValidateKey` via thunks
`FUN_112547c0`/`FUN_112547f0`/`FUN_112548b0` -> vtable `PTR_PTR_117531f4`:

- **One backend object, lazily created** `if (PTR_PTR_117531f4==0) PTR_PTR_117531f4 = &PTR_PTR_11759160` (only writes to
  `PTR_PTR_117531f4` are in `FUN_112547f0` and `FUN_112548b0`, both set it to that SAME default). Object `0x11759160` ->
  **vtable V = `0x116c6e48`**.
- **Vtable slots:** `+0` `FUN_112544f0`; **`+4` `FUN_11254be0`** (usedby `FUN_112547c0` = ValidateKey thunk);
  **`+8` `FUN_11254ae0`** (usedby `FUN_112547f0`); `+12` `FUN_11254c40` (usedby `FUN_112548b0`); `+32` `FUN_11256190` (ref-count).
- **The algorithm is MD5, NOT a signature:** `FUN_11254be0`, `FUN_11254c40` reference
  `RendezVous/./OnlineCore/src/Core/Checksum/MD5/MD5Checksum.h` and call `FUN_112378a0(...,mode=2)` (compute+compare);
  `FUN_11254ae0` (derive) indexes with `param_3 % this->[8] + this->[4]` and calls `FUN_1122df00`. There is **no
  asymmetric/RSA key and no baked Ubisoft secret** - it is MD5 over data both peers can hold.
- **Gate** `DAT_1174d35a` (picks the ValidateKey branch) is set to `0` by game init `FUN_10431d9a` (which also installs
  `DAT_11f06688=FUN_104319c7`, `DAT_11f0668c=FUN_104319d8` and configures timeouts via `FUN_11232a60/FUN_11232a80`).
- Selector `DAT_11f07f1c` selects the derive sub-branch in `FUN_112547f0`.

**Finalized patch (B) surface (all in-module, no crypto to break):**
1. **Inject** the ticket in `ProcessResponse` `FUN_1124d660` (out buffer `**(param_1+0x78)`) with a deterministic value derived
   from the session id / a shared password - and skip the network in `PrepareCall` `FUN_1124e5e0`.
2. **Neutralize the single validate check**: make the MD5 compare in vtable+4 `FUN_11254be0` (dispatched by `FUN_112547c0`)
   always succeed - OR overwrite vtable slot `+4`/`+8` of `0x116c6e48`, OR (cleanest, non-destructive) set `PTR_PTR_117531f4`
   to a custom backend vtable providing a deterministic `derive`(+8)/`validate`(+4). This is the "backend swap signature":
   the object pointer lives only in `PTR_PTR_117531f4` and is never read from config, so a loader/DLL can repoint it at init.
- **Feasibility verdict:** because the scheme is MD5-only, a patched host can derive the ticket locally and every patched
  peer validates the same MD5 - **no RendezVous service, no key to break.** Route B is confirmed the cheap, viable offline
  co-op path (contingent on the LAN-ticket question from the `fc3_lan_coop_launch.bat` experiment; if LAN join bypasses
  tickets entirely, even step 2 may be unnecessary).


### Session 28aa - ticket input provenance traced to JobTicketManagerLogin::InitiateLogin; whole pipeline is RV-connection gated

Ghidra `DecompileInputs`/`Decompile3`/`Decompile4` on `FC3d3d11_live` (46bcc097). Provenance of the deterministic ticket inputs:

- **Producer = InitiateLogin job builder `FUN_1124f5e0`** (the function holding the "JobTicketManagerLogin::InitiateLogin"
  string). It takes `this` + identity args `param_2..param_10` and stores them into the login job (`param_1` is a DWORD*,
  byte offsets): `+0x68`<-p2, `+0x6c`<-p3, **`+0xF0`<-a COPY of the string p6**, `+0xF4`<-p7, `+0xF8`<-p9, `+0xFC`<-p8,
  `+0x100`<-p10 (variant selector), `+0x194`<-p4. This bundle IS the per-login deterministic input set (the account / game /
  session / playlist / password identity the RV login provider hands in at join/create).
- **Driver = ProcessLoginResult `FUN_1124f000`**: passes `+0x194, +0xec, +0xf4, +0x180, +0xfc, +0xf8` (and selects the
  `+0x100==0` variant) into the message builder `FUN_1125a200` / `FUN_1125a3b0`.
- **Builder `FUN_1125a200`** serializes the request length-prefixed: `FUN_112232e0(len)`, a 4-byte id from `this+0x40`,
  literal `1`, `FUN_1125def0(str)` for the string field, then `FUN_1122b2c0` five times for the numeric fields; sends via
  `FUN_11258f20`. Then the MD5 backend runs: derive `FUN_11254ae0` / validate `FUN_11254be0` over
  `idx = param_3 % obj[8] + obj[4]` (obj = job+0x78 sub-backend; `obj[4]/obj[8]` = a baked checksum table), with the buffer
  from `**job+0xF4` and index `job+0xF0`.
- **CRITICAL GATE:** the entire ticket pipeline sits behind **`FUN_11237670` = RendezVous-connected**: `AcquireTicket`
  `FUN_1124e7b0` returns 0 unless it is set, and the builder `FUN_1125a200` also returns 0 unless it is set. So with NO RV
  connection the ticket path NEVER executes.

**Consequences for the two open questions:**
1. Deterministic shared key: both peers get the same `GameSessionKey` iff they feed the same InitiateLogin bundle
   (job offsets `+0x68,+0x6c,+0xF0(str),+0xF4,+0xF8,+0xFC,+0x100,+0x194`). Those args originate from the shared join
   parameters, so a deterministic local derive is achievable from data the lobby already shares. The last mile to NAME each
   field (session id vs password vs playlist vs account) = decompile the caller of `FUN_1124f5e0` (the RdV login provider job) - not yet done.
2. The RV gate `FUN_11237670` is the real fork: if the LAN/loopback join never opens an RV connection (likely, matching
   "offline flow never drives it"), this whole ticket pipeline is simply skipped -> co-op join needs no ticket at all, and
   patch (B) reduces to driving `NetEngineType=LAN` + `CFCXJoinSessionOperation`. If LAN join DOES connect RV, then (B) must
   also fake `FUN_11237670` and supply the bundle. The `fc3_lan_coop_launch.bat` experiment settles which.


### Session 28ab - InitiateLogin provider chain; identity is carried OPAQUELY from JobBackEndServicesLogin context (naming needs the game-side writer)

Ghidra `Decompile5`/`Decompile6` on `FC3d3d11_live`. Went 3 levels above the ctor to find who supplies the ticket identity:

- **Chain (all RV-gated by `FUN_11237670`):** `JobBackEndServicesLogin::ProcessAuthenticationResult` `FUN_11252710`
  (string `.../RendezVous/Core/src/Client/JobBackEndServicesLogin.cpp`) -> TicketManager facade `FUN_1124f7f0`
  (string `.../Authentication/TicketManager.cpp`) -> InitiateLogin ctor `FUN_1124f5e0` -> job fields.
- `FUN_11252710` issues the call reading its **login-context object** `param_1` (the `JobBackEndServicesLogin` job), e.g.
  `FUN_1124f7f0( param_1+0xd8, param_1+0x150, param_1+0x6c, *(param_1+0x70), <ptr+0x20>, param_1+0x1cc,
  *(param_1+0x27c), *(param_1+0x280) )`. So the identity bundle lives in the login-context fields
  **`{+0x6c ptr, +0x70 val, +0x150 ptr, +0x1cc ptr, +0x27c val, +0x280 val}`** (plus `+0x17c`->sub->`+0x20` and `+0x128/+0x12c/+0x290/+0x294`
  read for the guard), and is forwarded **unchanged** through the two TicketManager wrappers into the InitiateLogin job fields
  `{+0x68,+0x6c,+0xF0(str copy),+0xF4,+0xF8,+0xFC,+0x100,+0x194}`.
- **These RV-core functions are transparent forwarders** - they do not interpret the values, so at this layer there is no code
  that says "this is the session id / password / playlist id / account id". The names are assigned by the GAME-side producer
  that FILLS the `JobBackEndServicesLogin` context fields (the `CSessionLoginOperationProvider_RdV` / `CFCX*JoinSessionOperation`
  boundary), which is above the RV core and writes to a heap struct (not directly xref-able as a named field).

**Best inference (FLAGGED, not confirmed by a naming site):** `+0xF0`/`+0x150` is the only copied **string** -> the identity /
ticket string (account or user ticket); the numerics `{+0x6c,+0x70,+0x27c,+0x280}` map to the RV login ints - most plausibly
**account id / game id / playlist id / a login-variant flag** (the named session fields `SessionId`/`PlaylistId`/`AccountType`/
`SessionType` from the op schema `FUN_1061e581` are the candidates). The MD5 derive/validate use `idx = job+0xF0` over
`**job+0xF4`, so `+0xF4` is the buffer hashed together with the id.

**To convert inference into fact (next concrete trace):** find the write sites into the `JobBackEndServicesLogin` context
offsets `+0x150 / +0x1cc / +0x27c / +0x280` (i.e. the code that builds a `JobBackEndServicesLogin` from the CSession/CFCX join
parameters). That producer binds each named session field to a ticket input, and it is where the "password / playlist id /
account id" semantics actually live. This is the true remaining mile; the RV core itself only passes them through.


### Session 28ac - playbook networking review + DECISIVE: co-op session layer is uniformly RV-gated (no LAN bypass), route B = session-establishment shim

Read the hub `playbook/networking.md`. Then a decisive static check on the co-op join path.

**Playbook takeaways that apply to FC3 (recorded for reuse):**
- §3: "Always keep the LAN/direct-IP path working; every other option reduces to it and it is the easiest to test." -> LAN-first (our route).
- §3 VPN overlay (Tailscale/ZeroTier): "Works for every game with an IP field, no code." Attractive for the "online friends without
  port forwarding" half - **but see the FC3 caveat below: it only works if the game can host/join a LAN session; FC3's co-op session
  layer cannot, so the overlay alone is not enough.**
- §4 "Wrapping a game's existing UDP netcode (local proxy)" (HXCoop pattern): client proxy binds `127.0.0.1:<free>`, the game is told
  to join that address and believes it is a LAN server; host proxy opens one UDP socket per remote peer. This is the Transport-B /
  relay recipe for the data plane - but it needs a game session that accepts remote clients (see caveat).

**Decisive static result (`join_decompile.txt`, FC3_d3d11 46bcc097):** the co-op **session establishment** is uniformly RendezVous-bound,
both flavors, no LAN branch:
- Host path `FUN_11275f40`: **unconditionally** calls the gate `FUN_11279ba0`; if it returns 0 the join fails. On success -> `FUN_11275d90`
  (SubAccount + RV session).
- Online path `FUN_1127a300`: **same** `FUN_11279ba0` gate, then `FUN_1127a1d0` (UbiAuthentication).
- The gate `FUN_11279ba0` = `FUN_11245090()` (global online-enable flag; 0 -> immediate fail) **AND** `FUN_112492a0` (RV net-op that
  needs a resolved `SandboxUrl` address). Both are dead without Ubisoft, so neither flavor forms a session.
- The net-engine LAN/Online/Offline mode (`FUN_1092d256`, `param[0xe]` 0=LAN/1=Online/2=Offline) is **transport init only** and does not
  reach the RV gate - it cannot be used to bypass the session gate. The `storm::echo` LAN classes exist (28w) but nothing in the co-op
  session path drives them.

**Corrected plan / optimal route:**
- The VPN-overlay "no-code" shortcut is NOT viable for FC3 out of the box, because the co-op session gate (`FUN_11279ba0`) blocks before
  any LAN session. Overlay is only useful *after* the session layer can form a LAN session.
- **Route B is therefore a session-establishment SHIM, not just the ticket patch:** replace/short-circuit `FUN_11279ba0` so it (a) does not
  require the online flag or `SandboxUrl`, (b) returns success with a locally-created `storm::echo` session (via `NetEngineType=LAN` +
  `CFCXCreateSessionOperation`/`CFCXJoinSessionOperation`/`CSessionSearchIPOperation`, `LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable`), handing
  the resulting handle to the out param so `FUN_11275d90`/`FUN_1127a1d0` proceed over echo. On top of that, the RV-gated ticket pipeline
  (`FUN_11237670`/JobTicketManager, MD5-only, 28z) must be satisfied by the same-mod local derive.
- Patch surface (localized, all in-module): gate `FUN_11279ba0` (+ its gate inputs `FUN_11245090`/`FUN_112492a0`), session ops
  `CFCXCreate/JoinSessionOperation` + `NetEngineType`, and ticket `FUN_11254be0`/`ProcessResponse` (28z/28aa). No asymmetric crypto anywhere.
- Deployment for "friends without port forwarding" (post-shim, once LAN sessions form): Tailscale/ZeroTier overlay (no code) or the HXCoop
  local-proxy+relay (§4) over Steam relay. Overlay first because it is zero-code.


### Session 28ad - route B precise shim spec: the post-gate session handlers are themselves RV services (SubAccount / UbiAuth)

Ghidra `DecompileShim` on `FC3d3d11_live`. Going one level past the gate `FUN_11279ba0` shows stubbing the gate alone is NOT enough -
the co-op session handlers it releases into are themselves RendezVous service calls:

- **Host handler `FUN_11275d90`**: builds a `SubAccountClient` session (string `.../RendezVous/Services/SubAccount/src/Client/
  SubAccountClient.cpp`, via `FUN_11254400`, vtable `&PTR_FUN_116c6e2c`) after `FUN_11210d90`/`FUN_11214010` RV setup helpers. So hosting
  a co-op session is a **SubAccount** service op, not a plain storm::echo session.
- **Join handler `FUN_1127a1d0`**: the UbiAuthentication path (28t) - also RV service.
- **Gate out-handle = an address+port, sourced from RV**: `FUN_11232ef0` returns/uses `ntohs(*p + 2)` (a sockaddr port) and `FUN_11232e20`
  the address; the gate `FUN_11279ba0` fills `*param_2` from `FUN_11232ef0` after `FUN_112492a0` resolved the RV `SandboxUrl` host. So the
  peer address the game dials is **RV-provided**, not LAN-derived.

**Precise route-B shim (larger than the ticket patch, still localized, still no crypto secret):**
1. Replace the two co-op session handlers with raw storm::echo session ops: `FUN_11275d90` (host) -> `CFCXCreateSessionOperation`,
   `FUN_1127a1d0` (join) -> `CFCXJoinSessionOperation` (and/or `CSessionSearchIPOperation`), with `NetEngineType=LAN` and
   `LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable=1`, instead of the SubAccount / UbiAuthentication RV calls.
2. Supply the peer address: the game reads it from the gate `FUN_11279ba0` (address+port via `FUN_11232e20`/`FUN_11232ef0`); the shim must
   return a LAN address there (or drive `CFCXJoinSessionOperation -ip` directly), bypassing `FUN_112492a0`/`SandboxUrl`.
3. Bypass the gate preconditions `FUN_11245090` (online flag) / `FUN_112492a0` (RV/SandboxUrl).
4. Satisfy the RV-gated ticket pipeline `FUN_11237670`/JobTicketManager (MD5-only, 28z/28aa) via a same-mod local derive.

So the whole co-op session establishment (gate + host/join handlers + address + ticket) is RendezVous-shaped, and route B is a
**session-establishment shim** that swaps those four RV touchpoints for storm::echo session ops + a local address + a self-consistent
MD5 ticket. No asymmetric crypto anywhere; all four sites are in the running module. This is the actionable route-B spec.

### Session 28af - gate out-struct is a sockaddr_in; online global + rv-connected identified (scaffold filled)

Ghidra `DecompileAbi`. Concrete ABIs now fill the `src/coop_shim` hook bodies:

- **Online flag is a bare global**: `FUN_11245090` = `return DAT_11f07e48;`. So the shim just writes `DAT_11f07e48 = 1`
  (RVA `0x1f07e48`) at init - no hook strictly needed (kept the hook too as belt-and-braces).
- **Gate out-struct = classic `sockaddr_in`**: the peer the game dials is read by `FUN_11232ef0` (`ntohs(*(p+2))` = port at +2,
  network order) and `FUN_11232e20` (`ntohl(*(p+4))` + `inet_ntoa` = IPv4 at +4, network order; also `this[0x12]`/`FUN_1122f870`
  init). So `family@+0 (u16)=AF_INET(2)`, `port@+2 (u16, network)`, `addr@+4 (u32, network)`. The `hk_gate` replacement must build
  exactly that (documented inline with the `htons/htonl` recipe).
- **`FUN_11237670` ("RendezVous connected")** is not a simple flag - it checks a live connection/session object (via `FUN_1043341b`,
  `FUN_112345d0`) and is required by `AcquireTicket` (`FUN_1124e7b0`) + the ticket builder (`FUN_1125a200`). So it must be **hooked to
  return 1** (added `hk_rv_connected`, RVA `0x1237670`).
- **`FUN_112492a0`** reads config `"SandboxUrl"` (`FUN_11213a10`) and resolves an address list (`FUN_11249220`, then `FUN_11233110`/
  `FUN_11235be0`); returning 1 from the hook skips that RV resolution.

**Ticket MD5 inputs (still the one open field set):** the derive (`FUN_11254ae0`) / validate (`FUN_11254be0`) index a baked checksum
table by `idx = param_3 % obj[8] + obj[4]` with buffer `**job+0xF4`, index `job+0xF0` (NOTES 28aa). The semantic names of those
InitiateLogin-context fields (session id / playlist / account / password) are still only inferred (RV core forwards them opaquely), so
the scaffold's `derive_local_ticket` currently hashes only `FC3COOP_SECRET` - a real run must confirm the exact shared fields so both
peers derive the same key.

Scaffold updated and re-compiles clean (`build_x86.bat` -> BUILD_DONE 0).


### Session 28ag - op registry is a C++ name factory (not hand-callable); correct lever = force NetEngineType=LAN + short-circuit RV auth

Ghidra `DecompileReg`/`DecompileReg2`/`DecompileNet`. Question: can `hk_host`/`hk_join` create storm::echo `CFCXCreate/JoinSessionOperation` directly? **No** - and the correct lever is simpler.

Op registry mechanism (why not hand-callable):
- `FUN_1008bf50(this, "CFCXCreateSessionOperation", factory)` -> `FUN_1008bff8(name, factory, cnt)` appends the create-factory into a
  fixed op-type **descriptor array** (RTTI-style, no `strcmp` in the cluster - it is not a name->factory hash). Each op type is a static
  lazy-registered descriptor holding name + a create-factory vtable: `CFCXCreateSessionOperation` -> `&DAT_11e6b208` (via `FUN_107de8fa`),
  `CFCXJoinSessionOperation` -> `&DAT_11e74ee0` (via `FUN_1092e764`), `CSessionSearchIPOperation` -> `&DAT_11e42de8` (via `FUN_10425918`);
  all wrap base `CFCXGameOperation` -> `&DAT_11e68fd0` (via `FUN_107b2956`). The descriptors are referenced **only** by their lazy
  registrars, i.e. ops are created by the game through this descriptor's create-vtable + a param block + an async op lifecycle (refcount,
  completion callback). A 5-byte detour stub cannot safely reproduce that C++ lifecycle. So "replace host/join with hand-built CFCX ops"
  (the 28ad wording) is **wrong**; corrected below.

The RV auth handlers are not the session transport:
- `FUN_11275d90` (host) allocates a `SubAccountClient` (`FUN_11210af0(0x20, ".../SubAccountClient.cpp", 0x3b)`) and issues an RV service
  vtable call `(**(code**)(*param_1+0x14))(...)`; returns void, drives the RV async job.
- `FUN_1127a1d0` (join) gates on precondition `FUN_1127a0d0(...)` and builds a `UbiAuthenticationClient`
  (`FUN_11210af0(0x1a8, ".../UbiAuthenticationClient.cpp", 0x41)`) + RV vtable call `(**(code**)(*piVar3+4))()`; returns 1/0.
- Precondition `FUN_1127a0d0` returns 0 unless `FUN_11237670` (rv-connected) is true -> forcing it (28af hook) unlocks the join precondition.

The LAN path is first-class in the net engine (storm::echo), selected by a param, not by RV:
- `FUN_1092d256` (net-engine init) reads `param_1[0xe]`: `0="LAN"`, `1="Online"`, `2="Offline"` (it binds UDP; on failure logs
  "Net engine initialization failed.(UDP binding)"). So the transport mode is a plain init param.
- The join/create op **param block** (schema registered by `FUN_1061e581`) carries `NetEngineType` (field id `0x3c`), `SearchIdAddress`,
  `JoinSessionId` (28w). Setting `NetEngineType=0 (LAN)` makes the game build its own storm::echo `CFCXCreate/JoinSessionOperation` over LAN.

**Corrected shim design (Route B, concrete):** do NOT hand-build CFCX ops. Instead:
1. Force the net-engine/session op param `NetEngineType = 0 (LAN)` (`FUN_1092d256` `param[0xe]`; op param field id `0x3c`) so the game's
   own code creates the storm::echo session op.
2. Short-circuit the RV auth handlers `FUN_11275d90` (host) and `FUN_1127a1d0` (join) to no-op success so no `SubAccountClient`/
   `UbiAuthenticationClient` RV service calls are issued; the native LAN op then forms the session.
3. Keep the 28af levers: online global `DAT_11f07e48=1`, `FUN_11237670` rv-connected -> 1, `FUN_112492a0` -> 1, gate `FUN_11279ba0` ->
   success + LAN `sockaddr_in`, ticket validate -> accept local MD5.
Remaining runtime-only unknown: the exact op-param builder call site where `NetEngineType`/`SearchIdAddress` are written (to patch the
value there), and confirmation that the LAN `CFCXCreateSessionOperation` is reached once the RV handlers are neutralized - needs a live run.

### Session 28al - live HOST attempt: gate+join fire, host/ticket never; game aborts on UPLAY_USER_IsConnected / unfinished overlapped online op ("service unavailable")

Owner navigated the live game (shim injected, 28ak) to co-op online -> Host; the UI popped "Служба Far Cry 3 недоступна / Far Cry 3 service unavailable". `bin/coop_shim.log` (pid 7204, base 0x7a190000) shows exactly which hooks fired:

- Fired: `gate -> success` x3 and `join -> short-circuit RV auth` x3 (the co-op navigation reached the session gate + the join/session op).
- NEVER fired: `host_handler` (FUN_11275d90), `ticket_validate`, `ticket_prepare`.
- After those, the game loops `UPLAY_HasOverlappedOperationCompleted` forever. Cross-check of `bin/uplay_api.log` for the session shows the co-op-relevant Uplay calls are: `UPLAY_USER_IsConnected` (151x, the game repeatedly asking if the online user is connected), `UPLAY_GetOverlappedOperationResult` (fetching the async online op result), plus `UPLAY_PARTY_Init`/`UPLAY_FRIENDS_Init`/`UPLAY_ClearGameSession`/`UPLAY_USER_GetCredentials`.

Conclusion (confirms and sharpens 28k/28t, now with live trace): the online-coop Host path is gated at the **Uplay online-service layer** - the user-not-connected state (`UPLAY_USER_IsConnected`) and an async overlapped online op that never completes drive the "service unavailable" and stop Host **before** `host_handler` is ever called. The Route-B game-code detours (gate/host/join/ticket) are downstream of the actual wall and do not, by themselves, make co-op start.

What this does NOT rule out: whether Host uses `host_handler` at all (we only saw the gate+join stage; the "join" handler firing on a Host click suggests FC3 hosts via the same gate + session-search/join op, then would reach host creation only once the online op completes).

Next (the lever moved from FC3 code to the PROXY): to get past "service unavailable" the proxy must (a) report the online user connected (`UPLAY_USER_IsConnected` -> true) and (b) make the pending overlapped online op complete (`HasOverlappedOperationCompleted` -> true + a success `GetOverlappedOperationResult`), and possibly pump the login/party event. The current proxy fake only covers the 3 party gates and is otherwise transparent; so the next build step is to extend the proxy fake for `UPLAY_USER_IsConnected` + overlapped-op completion and re-test Host. (This is the NOTES 28k/28t "RV/PRUDP app layer is the true blocker" seen live from the client side.)


### Session 28am - proxy gate FC3COOP_FAKE_ONLINE: fake the Uplay online overlapped op; discovered IsConnected was already faked

Per the 28al live trace (blocker = the unfinished online-service overlapped op, NOT IsConnected), extended `src/uplay_proxy/uplay_proxy.cpp`:

- New gate `g_fakeonline` from env `FC3COOP_FAKE_ONLINE` (default OFF -> every export behaves exactly as before, no regression).
- When ON: `th_19` (UPLAY_HasOverlappedOperationCompleted) returns `g_fo_done` (=1 completed) instead of tail-calling real, and
  `th_18` (UPLAY_GetOverlappedOperationResult) returns `g_fo_result` (=0, the UPLAY success code) instead of tail-calling real. The values are
  plain tunable statics (`g_fo_done`/`g_fo_result`) so a re-test can flip them (e.g. result 1 vs 0) and rebuild.
- **Discovery:** `th_55` (UPLAY_USER_IsConnected) ALREADY does `mov eax,1; ret` unconditionally (like th_29/30) - so in the 28al Host run
  "connected" was ALREADY faked true, and co-op still failed with "service unavailable". This confirms the wall is the async online overlapped op
  (HasOverlapped never completing), not IsConnected; that is exactly what the new th_18/th_19 fakes target.
- ABI note/caveat: the fakes use a plain `ret` (cdecl, caller-cleans), matching the existing th_29/30/55 fakes. If HasOverlapped/GetResult are
  actually stdcall this could unbalance the stack - it is test-gated (off by default) and will show immediately if wrong.
- `scripts/deploy_coop_shim.bat` launcher now also sets `FC3COOP_FAKE_ONLINE=1` (with a rem; comment it out for a plain run). Fixed a bug where
  an echo line with `()` inside the launcher `(...)` block truncated the generated launcher.
- Built green: `build_x86.bat` -> `BUILD_DONE 0`, `uplay_r1_loader.dll` 140288 B. NOT deployed to the game (owner re-runs deploy + Host).

### Session 28ao - LIVE Host with FAKE_ONLINE=1: return-code faking is exhausted; the gate is the Uplay login/credential RESULT payload

Owner navigated co-op Host on pid 38212 with the new proxy (`proxy up ... fake=1 online=1`, `coop_shim.dll` loaded). Result: same "Far Cry 3 service unavailable" (RU dialog), V1.05 UI.

coop_shim.log: `gate` fired 6x (returned our LAN success), `join` fired 6x (RV-auth short-circuit). `host_handler` / `ticket_*` / `rv_connected` NEVER fired.

uplay_api.log (whole session, 75 calls, current pid): the co-op login burst is `UPLAY_ClearGameSession -> UPLAY_USER_GetCredentials -> UPLAY_USER_GetCdKeys -> HasOverlappedOperationCompleted x2 -> GetOverlappedOperationResult x2` (the credentials + cdkeys overlapped ops). Our `th_19` (HasOverlapped->done) IS taking effect: the game polls the op ONCE and moves on instead of looping forever (vs 28al). `IsConnected` forced 1 every time. Then NO co-op/party/lobby call ever follows; on the Host click the game made ZERO Uplay calls and just raised "service unavailable" -> it checked its OWN cached login/auth state, not a fresh call.

ROOT CAUSE: `UPLAY_GetOverlappedOperationResult` writes the operation RESULT (the credentials blob / cdkey data) through an OUT-POINTER, and our `th_18` fakes only the RETURN value, leaving the out-buffer empty. The game reads an invalid/empty credential+cdkey payload, its login/auth state stays invalid, and the online entry aborts with "service unavailable" BEFORE any party/lobby/host code - so `hk_host` can never be reached. Confirmed: the 58 proxy exports contain NO `UPLAY_PARTY_Create`/host/join call; FC3 forms the co-op session via RendezVous + `USER_SetGameSession`, all gated on the Uplay online login/credential state.

IMPLICATION: passing "service unavailable" by return-CODE faking is impossible - it needs VALID Uplay login payloads (the GetCredentials/GetCdKeys out-buffer contents + AccountId/identity). That is Ubisoft online-DRM payload synthesis (heavy RE, possibly identity/crypto validated) - the exact online-service wall NOTES 28t predicted. The cheap lever is done.

NEXT LEVERS (deeper, static RE; agent picks B then A):
- (B) find the online-vs-LAN branch: locate the code/string that raises "service unavailable" and the predicate around the co-op Host entry; if FC3 has a LAN/`storm::echo` path (Bantila "1.05 LAN") that skips the Uplay login gate, routing to it makes (A) unnecessary.
- (A) if no LAN bypass: reverse the consumer of `GetOverlappedOperationResult` (caller `FC3_d3d11.dll+0x7acb19`) + the credential/cdkey buffer layout + `UPLAY_USER_GetAccountId`, and synthesize a valid "logged-in" payload in the proxy. Risk: identity/crypto checks may make it unwinnable.


### Session 28ap - option B: FC3 DOES have native LAN co-op hosting in storm::echo; how to reach it (replaces the "fake Uplay login" plan)

Static RE (read-only) for option B. The co-op "service unavailable" is `E_SESSION_ERROR_GATOR_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` (string `0x116ee7f4`, name table switch at `0x113abfd0..0x113ac068`). The GATOR error family around it is all ONLINE-session AUTH errors: `E_SESSION_ERROR_GATOR_INVALID_TICKET / INVALID_TOKEN / EXPIRED_TOKEN / KEY_DECRYPTION_FAILED / SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` - i.e. the online session service (RendezVous + GATOR) is authentication-gated, which is exactly why return-code faking (28ao) could not pass it.

Matchmaking provider classes (RTTI/name strings, referenced from `.rdata` tables, not by imm32): `CGameMatchmakingProvider` (`0x11589231`) base, `CGameMatchmakingProvider_RdV` (`0x1158b43d`) = the ONLINE (RendezVous/GATOR) provider the co-op menu uses. Account feature-flag names (`0x11526af4..0x11526b58`): `EAccountFlags_Rendezvous | Gator | Live | LAN | Offline`; the LAN bit is tested at `0x1044400a` (`test bl,0x02`). Engine has offline states: `NetEngineType` (`0x115615fc`) and `ENetEngineState_Initialized_Online` vs `_Offline`, `ENetEngineState_Initializing_Online` vs `_Offline`.

Native LAN hosting/discovery is fully present in the netcode (storm::echo). `net_` group CVars (registered around `0x10103e80`; the LAN-matchmaking pair read at `0x107a25xx`):
`LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable` (discover sessions on the local machine only, no broadcast - loopback), `LanMatchmakingReuseAddress` (SO_REUSEADDR so many apps can host sessions on one machine), `LanHostBroadcastPort`, `LanClientBroadcastPort`, `ScanPortStart`, `ScanPortRange`, `ScanFreePorts`, `LanMin/Initial/MaxUploadInbps`. This is a provider-agnostic LAN path with no GATOR and no Uplay login.

DECISIVE: the session/search parameter binder (`~0x1061e600`) binds named fields to struct field-ids: `SessionRefId`=0x20, `SessionType`=0x38, `NetEngineType`=**0x3c** (corroborates the 28ag Route-B field id), `AccountType`=0x40, `MatchMakingEnabled`=**0x4d** (bool setter `0x102629b5`), `PlaylistId`=0x34, `DeleteMessageId`=0x30, plus `Search*` fields.

ANSWER to "how to exit to LAN": force the co-op session PARAM to the LAN provider instead of RdV - set `NetEngineType` (id 0x3c) to the offline/LAN value and `MatchMakingEnabled` (id 0x4d) to 0 on the CSessionParam, so `CSession...CreateSession` runs the storm::echo LAN path (broadcast or `LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable` for a same-machine test, `LanMatchmakingReuseAddress` for several clients on one box) and never contacts GATOR -> no "service unavailable" and `host_handler`/`ticket_*` become reachable. This supersedes faking the Uplay login payload (28ao path A).

OPEN ITEMS (next static steps): (1) the exact `NetEngineType` enum value for LAN/offline (read the `ENetEngineType` enum / the switch that maps type->provider, e.g. near the `_RdV` vtable); (2) the co-op menu has no LAN entry, so the param must be forced in code (detour the param builder / op-param `FUN_1092d256` writes param[0xe]=0 for LAN), not via the UI; (3) confirm the JOIN side drives LAN discovery via `ScanPortStart/Range` + `LanClientBroadcastPort`.



### Session 28aq - option B refined: the co-op Host gate is a UbiAuthentication/RendezVous helper; FC3 CAMPAIGN CO-OP is hardwired to online (no co-op LAN entry)

Disassembling the already-firing co-op gate `FUN_11279ba0` (VA `0x11279ba0`) shows what it actually is:
- `call 0x11245090` -> esi = the online/RV context (the `DAT_11f07e48` global getter the shim hooks); `esi==0` -> return 0 (fail).
- builds a struct, `call 0x112492a0` (`rv_sandboxurl`, also hooked) for the RV sandbox URL, then the cluster at `0x11279c20..0x11279c9a` all push the string `'UbiAuthenticationLoginCustomData'` (`0x116cf7d0/f7f4/f818`) -> this is the **UbiAuthenticationClient** login-custom-data accessor chain.
- finally `mov word ptr [edx], ax` writes the port field (the value the shim's "LAN 127.0.0.1:9000" fake supplies) and returns 1.

So the gate is INSIDE the UbiAuthentication/RendezVous blob-builder, not an online-vs-LAN branch. Conclusion (correcting/finishing 28ap): the storm::echo LAN matchmaking primitives (NetEngineType Online/Offline, `LanMatchmaking*`, `EAccountFlags_LAN`) are the MULTIPLAYER / dedicated-server session layer; FC3 **campaign co-op** ("Co-op -> Host") is hardwired to the online provider (`UbiAuthenticationClient` + RendezVous + GATOR) with NO LAN menu/code entry. That is why our gate success still ends in `E_SESSION_ERROR_GATOR_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` - downstream the code needs the real UbiAuthentication/RV blob.

Therefore the two live routes are unchanged in kind, and the LAN shortcut does not exist for co-op:
- (A) synthesize the UbiAuthentication/RV/GATOR login payload the co-op path consumes (28ao A) - heavy, possibly crypto/identity-gated.
- (B') force the co-op `CSessionParam` onto the LAN storm::echo engine (NetEngineType id 0x3c = offline/LAN + MatchMakingEnabled id 0x4d = 0) at the session-creation op, so RV/GATOR is skipped entirely - but the co-op session layer is built around RV, so B' must override the op that currently supplies RV params (the gate cluster above). Uncertain whether the co-op session node accepts a non-RV provider at all.

Decision for the owner/Bantila: ask directly whether "1.05 LAN" is REAL campaign-co-op-over-LAN (which provider did he force?) or MP-only LAN. That answer selects A vs B'. Next static step for B': find the co-op session-creation op that fills the CSessionParam (callers of the param binder region `~0x1061e600`) and the `NetEngineType` writer, to see if it can be detoured to LAN. PR #1 updated with this finding and a precise question to Bantila.



### Session 28ar - option B' structural answer: co-op host is polymorphic over a session provider; no in-path online/lan branch; LAN swap = provider-object swap at init

Disassembled the co-op Host path in `FC3_d3d11.dll`:

Flow: a wrapper at `0x11275f40` runs the gate `FUN_11279ba0` (`UbiAuthenticationLoginCustomData` blob builder) as a PRE-CHECK (`test al,al; jne` at `0x11275f67`); only on success does it build the arg struct and `call FUN_11275d90` (the real host handler). Join mirrors this (gate call at `0x1127a31b`). Our shim makes the gate return success, so the pre-check passes and `FUN_11275d90` runs and hits RV.

Inside `FUN_11275d90` (host handler):
- It constructs a **RendezVous SubAccountClient**: pushes the internal source path string (`.rdata` `0x116ce2a8` = `.../online/RendezVous/RendezVous/Services/SubAccount/src/Client/SubAccountClient.cpp`) into a constructor at `0x11210af0`, and assigns RV-object vtables (`0x116c6e2c`, `0x116ce3d0`). (Internal Ubisoft Perforce path observed in the binary - kept local, not for the PR.)
- It then creates the host session through a **POLYMORPHIC provider**: `mov edx,[esi]; mov edx,[edx+0x14]; call edx` at `0x11275e7e` (a virtual method of the controller object `esi=this`), plus a `call 0x112536a0` with a class-name descriptor pointer `0x115279f4` (inside the `CSessionCreateOperation*` name pool).

So there is NO online-vs-LAN `if` inside the co-op host code: the provider object (its vtable) decides RV vs LAN. `NetEngineType` (id 0x3c) / `MatchMakingEnabled` (id 0x4d) are declared in the param type-descriptor (one-time reflection registration at `~0x1061e580`, guarded by init flag `0x11e5d612`), and per-session values are assigned via field-id setters by the FCX create-session op (`CFCXCreateSessionOperation` / `CSessionCreateGameOperation`).

Verdict on B': feasible in PRINCIPLE but NOT a flag flip. The netcode has a non-RdV base `CSessionCreateOperation` (`0x11523fa8`) separate from `CSessionCreateOperation_RdV` (`0x11527a78`), i.e. a native storm::echo (LAN-capable) session provider EXISTS, and the co-op host/join handlers are polymorphic over it -> the co-op node COULD run on the LAN provider. The detour is therefore: make the session controller construct/attach the **LAN/base provider object instead of the RendezVous one** at controller init (this is the `NetEngineType` decision that gives `esi` its vtable), NOT to patch a branch in the host call.

Blocker for the exact detour: RTTI is stripped in this build (no `.?AV...` names), so the provider-construction switch (where the controller chooses base vs `_RdV`, driven by `NetEngineType`) is not reachable by name xref. Best next step is a runtime logging detour on the provider virtual factory (`this->vtable[+0x14]`, i.e. the `call edx` at `0x11275e7e`) and/or dumping `esi`'s vtable at Host, to (a) capture the RV provider vtable, (b) find the base/LAN provider vtable (compare to the `CSessionCreateOperation` base), and (c) force-rewrite `esi`'s vtable pointer to the LAN provider. That is a shim change + one Host run (owner UI).



### Session 28as - shim provider-factory call-site probe added (FC3COOP_HOOK_PROVIDER); run the real host path to capture the provider vtable

Implemented the 28ar next-step in `src/coop_shim/coop_shim.cpp`: a CALL-SITE hook on the polymorphic provider dispatch inside host_handler.

- Site: `RVA_provider_factory_site` = 0x01275e7e (`FF D2` = `call edx`, i.e. `edx = *(this->vtable + 0x14)`), resume at `RVA_provider_factory_back` = 0x01275e86.
- Env `FC3COOP_HOOK_PROVIDER=1`: (a) SKIPS the `host_handler` short-circuit so the REAL host path runs and reaches the dispatch, (b) installs `patch_provider_factory()`.
- The detour is a naked trampoline: `pushad` -> `log_provider_factory(esi=this, edx=method)` -> `popad` -> re-execute the ORIGINAL `call edx` (same regs/stack) -> replay the displaced `mov ecx,[ebp-0x128]` -> `jmp dword ptr [g_provider_jmpback]` (g_provider_jmpback = g_mod + back, so ASLR-safe). The 5-byte patch covers the 2-byte `call edx` + the first 3 bytes of the following `mov ecx,[ebp-0x128]`; we skip back to +8 and re-run the whole 6-byte mov from the trampoline.
- Safety: before patching, verifies the site bytes are `FF D2` and the displaced bytes `8B 8D`, else logs and skips (wrong module -> no crash). `log_provider_factory` wraps reads in SEH `__try`.
- Log output: `this`, the provider vtable pointer, `vtable+0x14`, the vtable slots [0..7], and their RVAs vs module base -> lets the RV provider class vtable be identified and compared with the static non-RdV `CSessionCreateOperation`.

Verified: `build_x86.bat` -> `BUILD_DONE 0`, `coop_shim.dll` 131072 B; the trampoline bytes are present in the binary (`FF D2 8B 8D D8 FE FF FF FF 25`, exactly one occurrence). `scripts/deploy_coop_shim.bat` launcher now prints a commented `rem set FC3COOP_HOOK_PROVIDER=1`. Still NOT deployed.

How to capture: in `run_coop_shim.bat` uncomment `set FC3COOP_HOOK_PROVIDER=1`, deploy + run + go to Co-op -> Host. The game will STILL end at "service unavailable" (expected - the host path is real and RV-gated), but `bin/coop_shim.log` will contain `provider_factory: this=... vtable=... vtbl_rva=... slot_rva=...` -> that RVA is the RV provider vtable. Compare to the base `CSessionCreateOperation` (static 0x11523fa8 area) to locate the LAN provider vtable to force.



### Session 28at - provider probe armed+installed but never fired: offline Host aborts at the GATOR login wall BEFORE FC3 host_handler; the RV-vs-LAN decision is UPSTREAM of host_handler

Owner ran the FC3COOP_HOOK_PROVIDER=1 capture (pid 20892, base 0x79640000). coop_shim.log shows the probe installed correctly (`SKIP host_handler short-circuit` + `patch provider_factory @0x7a8b5e7e`) but NO `provider_factory:` lines -> the `call edx` inside host_handler `FUN_11275d90` was never reached. `uplay_api.log` for the run: only the normal startup burst (Init/PARTY_Init/GetCredentials/GetCdKeys/IsConnected polls/ACH) then `UPLAY_Quit`/`UPLAY_Release`; the Host click produced NO new party/session/storm calls.

So swapping the provider at `host_handler` is unreachable without first passing the online login (Route A), and the probe cannot capture the vtable via the Host button offline.

Corrected route: the real RV-vs-LAN decision is UPSTREAM of host_handler - at the co-op Host menu/FCX code that on Host-click first checks online state and picks the RendezVous path (which then leads to host_handler / the GATOR call). That upstream predicate/branch is the lever to force LAN (or to bypass the online pre-check), NOT host_handler. Next static step: find that Host-click decision point (callers leading to FUN_11275d90's wrapper 0x11275f40, and the online-state check that raises GATOR SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE on the co-op path) and identify where a non-RdV/base provider could be selected before the RV pre-check. Probe build stays (harmless; env-gated). NOT deployed further.



### Session 28au - DEFINITIVE static map: campaign co-op = a RendezVous/GATOR op cascade (no LAN backend); B/B' dead; realistic route = fake the op cascade + storm::echo LAN (in-game bridge)

Completing the upstream hunt (28at). The single caller of the host wrapper `0x11275f40` is `0x104436c6`, inside function `0x10443663`. That function is the co-op Host action and it BAILS EARLY if the backend singleton `[0x11e42c90]` is null (`cmp [0x11e42c90],0; je bail 0x1044372c`) or `this[0]`/arg null -> offline it never reaches the wrapper, which is exactly why the 28as provider probe never fired (the RV backend was never constructed because RV login failed).

Static architecture (all in FC3_d3d11.dll):
- Game-level OnlineSession BACKENDS are RendezVous-only: `RendezVousBackEndServices_native.cpp` (`0x115265d8`), `RendezVousGuestBackEndServices_native.cpp` (`0x115268e0`). No LAN/native co-op backend exists. Login-operation providers registered base `CSessionLoginOperationProvider` (`0x11526878`) + `CSessionLoginOperationProvider_RdV` (`0x11526898`).
- Co-op online is a FCX/RV OP CASCADE (each an op): `CFCXGatorLoginCheckOperation` (`0x115aba50`, error string "Could not get services." `0x115abed0`) -> `CRendezVousLoginOperation` (`0x11526744`) -> `CRendezVousConfigureUPnPOperation` (`0x11528268`) / `CRendezVousInitPunchOperation` (`0x115282dc`) -> `CSessionCreateOperation_RdV` (`0x11527a78`) -> host_handler `FUN_11275d90` (builds RV `SubAccountClient`). Errors `E_SESSION_ERROR_RENDEZVOUS_TRANSPORT_CONNECTION_FAILURE` (`0x115e4f20`), the GATOR family (28ap).
- RendezVous is FULLY STATICALLY LINKED into the game (`D:/Perforce/FC3/.../online/RendezVous/OnlineCore/...`, `BerkeleySocketDriver`) and talks to Ubisoft servers over its own sockets (host `fc3-bloomberg-server.ubisoft.org` `0x11503cd4`). Not via the proxied `uplay_r1_loader.dll` API.

VERDICT: (B) native LAN campaign co-op does NOT exist (backend is RV-only); (B') provider-swap at host_handler is unreachable offline (gated behind the RV login that fails). Both dead.

REALISTIC remaining route = the "in-game bridge" pattern (hub playbook in-game-bridge.md): fake the RV/FCX online OP CASCADE so the session forms, and carry the actual game traffic over storm::echo LAN (loopback / LanMatchmaking). Concretely, staged: (1) hook `CFCXGatorLoginCheckOperation` and `CRendezVousLoginOperation` execute/completion to post success result events; (2) hook `CSessionCreateOperation_RdV` (+ join) to create a storm::echo LAN session (NetEngineType=LAN, MatchMakingEnabled=0) instead of contacting GATOR; (3) feed the login-custom-data/credential fields the downstream builders read with synthetic values. This is substantial and iterative (many ops + result-event schemas), and is the honest shape of the work. The cheap levers are exhausted.

NEXT staged step: locate `CFCXGatorLoginCheckOperation`'s vtable/execute (first op in the cascade) as the earliest fakeable point, and inspect what result-event it must post. NOT deployed further; probe build (28as) stays env-gated.



### Session 28av - master gate identified: online session-manager singleton 0x11e42c90 gates ALL online incl. co-op; null offline; online-layer static RE is COMPLETE

Host-action `0x10443663` (co-op Host) has a single caller `0x10442046`. Its first gate is `cmp [0x11e42c90],0; je bail` (28au). `0x11e42c90` is read 271x across `.text` (a1/8b 0d/8b 3d forms) = the game-wide ONLINE SESSION MANAGER singleton, the master gate for every online feature (solo-online + co-op host/join all read it).

Where it is created (write site): `0x10420a77` does `mov ecx,[0x11e42a90]; call 0x10418a3d; mov edx,[eax]; call [edx+4]; mov [0x11e42c90],eax` - i.e. the manager = the result of a POLYMORPHIC vtable[+4] call on the object held in global `0x11e42a90`, performed during online initialization. Offline the online backend is not connected, that call yields nothing usable, `0x11e42c90` stays null, and every online path (co-op host action included) short-circuits to its bail - which is exactly the observed behaviour (no session/party calls on Host, provider probe never fires).

This is the hard DRM/online boundary: to run campaign co-op offline you must make `[0x11e42c90]` a VALID, initialized online session-manager. Two shapes: (A) make the real online manager initialize = complete a real/emulated Uplay+RendezVous login (28ao credential-payload wall); (A'') synthesize a fake manager object implementing the full vtable surface that the 271 call sites expect (very large, brittle). There is NO LAN/online co-op entry upstream of this gate (28aq/28au: RV-only backends, no LAN backend). All cheap levers are exhausted and the online-layer static analysis is complete: FC3 1.06 PC campaign co-op is architecturally hard-gated on a live/emulated Ubisoft online session-manager, with no LAN path.

DECISION FORK for the owner (agent cannot decide cost): (1) invest in (A)/(A'') heavy emulation; (2) Bantila reality-check - if his "1.05 LAN" campaign co-op truly works, either his build differs from 1.06 PC or he patched this exact `[0x11e42c90]` gate to a stub manager; ask him precisely which, that answer likely collapses the search; (3) pause. No further deployment; probe build (28as) stays env-gated, launcher still has FC3COOP_HOOK_PROVIDER=1 (deploy resets to commented).




### Session 28aw - manager-creation chain mapped; path A'' cost assessment; PARK pending Bantila

Fully traced the master-gate creation chain (28av):
- Online container singleton global `0x11e42a90` (RVA `0x1E42A90`), size 0x10, LAZY-created by factory at `0x100bc087` (`new(0x10)` via `0x10088f04` + ctor `0x104189f3`; single write site `0x100bc0a1`). Created on first access regardless of online status (it is just a container).
- Container field `+0x8` = the "session backend" object; trivial getter `0x10418a3d` = `mov eax,[ecx+8]; ret`.
- The master-gate singleton `[0x11e42c90]` (the online session manager) = `backend->vtable[+4]()` at `0x10420a77` (`mov ecx,[0x11e42a90]; call 0x10418a3d; mov edx,[eax]; call [edx+4]; mov [0x11e42c90],eax`). It is populated when online login succeeds; offline the backend returns nothing usable and it stays null -> every online path (incl. campaign co-op Host `0x10443663`) bails.

Cost assessment for the two offline shapes:
- (A) real/emulated Uplay+RendezVous login so the real backend yields a real session-manager: hits the credential out-pointer payload wall (28ao); needs the RV/GATOR protocol + login payload. Heavy.
- (A'') synthesize a fake session-manager object: must implement the manager's FULL interface. The global is read at ~250 sites and the manager is passed as `this` to many member calls (a vtable-slot histogram is not cheaply derivable because it is stored in `this` fields and dispatched far from the load) -> the surface is large and brittle. Not a quick shim.

CONCLUSION / PARK: static architecture of the FC3 1.06 PC online/co-op layer is COMPLETE and documented (28av/28aw). Offline campaign co-op is architecturally hard-gated on a live or fully-emulated Ubisoft online session-manager; no LAN path exists. The decision to invest in (A)/(A'') is a cost call for the owner, and the cheapest information is Bantila's answer to the posted PR #1 question (comment 6851368690): if he stubs this exact gate, his stub reveals the MINIMAL manager interface to fake and collapses the search. Recommendation: WAIT for that reply before spending on emulation. Deployment unchanged; launcher still has FC3COOP_HOOK_PROVIDER=1 (deploy resets to commented). No further changes made.




### Session 28ax - path A'' survey tool: online-session-manager vtable tracer built (FC3COOP_TRACE_MGR) + deployed; offline run procedure documented

Owner chose to evaluate path A'' (minimal fake online session-manager). Since the gate `[0x11e42c90]` (RVA `0x1E42C90`) is NULL offline, its methods are never called there, so the survey must run during a NORMAL Ubisoft online login (FC3COOP_FAKE_ONLINE OFF) when the real manager is created and used.

Built a runtime vtable tracer in the shim (env `FC3COOP_TRACE_MGR=1`):
- `mgr_tracer_thread` polls `*(base+0x1E42C90)` (only memory reads + Sleep) until the manager + its vptr are stable across two polls (~500ms apart, up to ~30 min).
- Reads the vtable and, for each slot whose value is a code pointer inside the module ([base, base+SizeOfImage)), replaces it with a generated stub: `pushad; push imm8 i; call mgr_log_slot; add esp,4; popad; jmp dword ptr [&g_mgr_orig[i]]`. This is stack-neutral and thiscall-`ecx`-preserving (tail call to the original), so the game keeps working while it is observed.
- `mgr_log_slot(i)` increments a per-slot counter and logs `MGR slot[+0x<off>] hit#<n> (method_rva=0x...)` for the first 3 hits of each slot. The SET of slot offsets that get hit = the set of manager methods the game actually calls = the minimal interface a fake manager must implement (path A'').
- vtable page made RW via VirtualProtect only for the patch window, then restored; stubs live in a VirtualAlloc RWX pool. Guarded by __try for the manager/vptr reads. Image size captured from the PE header at init.

Build: coop_shim.dll x86 BUILD_DONE 0, 133632 B (src/coop_shim/coop_shim.cpp). Deployed to `H:\FarCryCoop\TestFC3_105\bin` via deploy_coop_shim.bat (timestamped backups). Launcher `run_coop_shim.bat` rewritten to TRACER mode: `FC3COOP_SHIM=1 FC3COOP_TRACE_MGR=1`, FAKE_ONLINE/HOOK_PROVIDER commented (FAKE_ONLINE MUST stay off so the real login creates the manager).

RUN (owner): (1) make sure Ubisoft Connect is logged in; (2) run run_coop_shim.bat; (3) at the main menu online features initialize (the manager should appear -> the tracer logs `mgr=... vtable=... patched N slots`); (4) play a minute and go Co-op -> Host to force the co-op code to touch the manager too; (5) close the game and read `bin\coop_shim.log` lines `MGR slot[+0x..] hit#..` -> send to agent. Expected outcomes: (a) `patched N slots` + a spread of hits => we get the concrete manager interface to size the fake; (b) `manager never created (offline/no login)` => confirms even the game's own session-manager requires the live Ubisoft login (strengthens the 28av hard-gate conclusion). This is read/observe-only; no faking yet.




### Session 28ay - A'' survey RESULT: manager interface = only 16 called slots (many trivial accessors); co-op Host uses join_handler not host_handler; only hard gate is [0x11e42c90]!=0 -> try fake-zeroed manager (FC3COOP_FAKE_MGR)

Ran the 28ax tracer live (real Ubisoft login, FC3COOP_FAKE_ONLINE off). Manager created: `mgr=488748C0 vtable_rva=0x01527870` (an RV object, near CSessionCreateOperation_RdV 0x11527a78), **46 vtable slots**. Owner navigated online menu + Co-op -> Host.

Survey result - exactly **16 distinct slots are ever called** (capped 3 hits each):
`+0x0c,+0x1c,+0x24,+0x28,+0x2c,+0x30,+0x48,+0x4c,+0x50,+0x54,+0x70,+0x8c,+0x90,+0x94,+0x98,+0xa0`. Disassembly of several shows MANY are trivial accessors: `+0x28`(+0x4c) = `mov al,1; ret`; `+0x48` = `xor al,al; ret`; `+0x0c` = `return (ecx? &this+0x280 : 0)`; `+0x24`(0x1044a600) = `mov eax,[ecx+0x2b0]; ret`; `+0xa0`(0x1044a607) = `mov al,[ecx+0x2d5]; ret`. Only a couple (e.g. `+0x1c` 0x10449fe2) contain real logic (calls into this->+0x280 sub-object). So the manager is a large object (~0x2d8+ bytes with sub-objects at +0x110,+0x280,+0x2b0) but the co-op/menu hot path mostly hits cheap field getters.

NEW FACT: the shim `host_handler` hook (RVA 0x1275d90) NEVER fired, while `gate` fired 4x and `join_handler` (0x127a1d0) fired 4x. So the real Co-op -> Host menu path goes through the JOIN-side gate+handler (FUN_1127a1d0 / join_online flavor), NOT host_handler FUN_11275d90. (Corrects the 28ar/28as focus on host_handler as the host entry.)

STRATEGIC SYNTHESIS: with a real login the manager exists and the co-op action passes its `cmp [0x11e42c90],0` gate and reaches our hooks (gate/join fired). Offline it dies ONLY because the manager is null. Therefore the tractable A'' experiment is a FAKE manager: allocate a zeroed ~0x400-byte object + a vtable whose slots return benign values (safest: copy the real vtable's 46 original pointers - the cheap getters then just read the zeroed fields; override the few that would deref null sub-objects), and set `[0x11e42c90] = &fake_mgr` (env `FC3COOP_FAKE_MGR=1`). If the gate then passes and our existing LAN hooks (gate/host/join/ticket) carry the session, campaign co-op may form over loopback storm::echo WITHOUT any Ubisoft server. Risk: the non-trivial slots (+0x1c etc.) deref sub-pointers that are null -> may need stubbing per-crash (iterate). This is the next build. Game pid 1860 was left running for the owner.




### Session 28az - implemented FC3COOP_FAKE_MGR (path A'' experiment): zeroed fake online session-manager + watchdog; built 134144B, deployed; honest scope

Added a path-A'' experiment to the shim (env `FC3COOP_FAKE_MGR=1`):
- `install_fake_mgr()`: VirtualAlloc a zeroed 0x600-byte object; set its first dword (vptr) to `base + RVA_mgr_vtable` where `RVA_mgr_vtable = 0x1527870` (the real manager class vtable seen live in 28ay; it lives in .rdata so it is ASLR- and login-independent). Because the vptr points at the REAL vtable, the 16 hot slots keep correct stack discipline (proper `ret n`); the cheap getters (28ay: `mov al,1/0`, `mov eax,[ecx+0x2b0]`, `lea eax,[ecx+0x280]`) just read the zeroed fields.
- Writes `[0x11e42c90] = fake` immediately and starts a watchdog (`fake_mgr_thread`) that re-forces the fake ONLY when the global is 0 - so it never clobbers a real online manager (safe to run while online; it only fills in the offline case).

The purpose of an FAKE_MGR run is to learn: (1) does the game survive long enough on the zeroed fake to pass the manager gate WITHOUT crashing in one of the ~1 non-trivial slots (+0x1c 0x10449fe2) -> if it crashes there, override that vtable slot with a benign stub; (2) where does co-op then fail next (expected: the RV session-create op).

If the game instead shows "must connect to Ubisoft" at launch, the fake-manager route needs the RV/Uplay endpoints black-holed another way; that is an owner-environment decision.

NEXT fork (owner cost call): (1) run the offline FAKE_MGR probe to validate the fake-manager survives and see the next wall; (2) pivot to the higher-value op-level work (redirect the session-create provider to storm::echo LAN + wire the gate's sockaddr to loopback - the actual bridge); (3) wait for Bantila. ### Session 28bb - FAKE_MGR force probe result: NEGATIVE - zeroed fake manager crashes the game within 30s; path A'' at the manager level is not viable (too expensive); pivot to op-level bridge

FC3COOP_FAKE_MGR=2 (force always) probe result: the shim's fake-manager (zeroed 0x600 + real vtable pointers) was successfully installed and the watchdog started, but the game process died within ~30 seconds of FC3 loading. The game's online init actively calls manager methods (achievement polls, friend list, party state, etc.) that dereference real sub-objects/return real session data — on our zeroed fake these deref null → crash. FINAL CONCLUSION after 28ax/28ay/28bb: path A'' (synthesize a fake online session-manager to replace the null gate) is infeasible at reasonable cost. Path A (complete/emulated Uplay+RV login) hits the credential-payload wall (28ao). Both are too expensive relative to the payoff.

The ONLY realistic remaining engineering path = IN-GAME BRIDGE at the STORM::ECHO SESSION layer: KEEP the real manager (requires real Ubisoft online login, so this only works when Ubisoft is up) and redirect the session CREATE/JOIN op from the RendezVous provider to a storm::echo LAN (NetEngineType=0) provider. Specifically: hook the provider-factory call site (or force NetEngineType param in the create-op builder) so that the game forms the storm::echo session over LAN rather than contacting RV/GATOR. This lets two or more players on the SAME Ubisoft network (or the same PC loopback) form a co-op session without RV matchmaking. This path: (1) does NOT remove the DRM/login requirement (still needs Ubisoft Connect online, confirmed 28ba: the game can't even start offline); (2) only makes co-op go PEER-TO-PEER storm::echo instead of RV-relayed. This is the hub's standard in-game-bridge approach (playbook in-game-bridge.md). The concrete targets are the join_handler path (NOT host_handler, per 28ay) and the session-create op param builder (NetEngineType field id 0x3c, FUN_1092d256 param[0xe]=0=LAN). Next step (NOT yet started): identify where the _RdV provider is chosen for the storm::echo op, and force NetEngineType=LAN / swap provider pointer.

### Session 28bc - op-bridge hunt: campaign co-op join op is HARDWIRED to UbiAuth-login + RV (no engine-type parameter); "force NetEngineType=LAN" is not a thing here

Tried to find a "force storm::echo LAN" switch on the real co-op path (owner asked). Chain: join_handler FUN_1127a1d0 -> precondition FUN_1127a0d0 (0x1127a221) -> joinservice via FUN_11210af0 ("RendezVousGuestBackEndServices") -> **FUN_11279f90 = the join-op factory** (called 0x1127a293), then the op is started via virtual `vtable[+4]` at 0x1127a2a6.

Disassembly of FUN_11279f90 shows the op is built for RV/UbiAuth with NO engine/net-type parameter to flip:
- string `'h7fyctiuucf'` (0x116ced8c) = the RendezVous application/gateway key used for the auth,
- login with DUMMY credentials `'DummyUbiUser@quazal.com'` (0x116ceda4) / `'UbiDummyPwd'` (0x116ced98),
- the op sets `[esi]=0x116cf86c` (its active vtable) and `[ebp-0x14]='JobUbiAuthenticationLogin::PrepareLogin'` (0x116cee88), then starts via FUN_1121c8f0.
So the co-op join op's FIRST step is a **UbiAuthentication Login job** (with placeholder creds), then a RendezVous join. There is no NetEngineType/param[0xe] branch inside this op that selects LAN vs Online - the op is RV/UbiAuth by construction. The storm::echo LAN session ops (base CSessionCreateOperation / CSessionSearchIPOperation) are a SEPARATE op family the co-op menu never builds.

Implication: a one-field "force LAN" hook does not exist on this path. Options to get LAN/Ubisoft-free co-op, all large:
(A) Keep the real path but rely on real UbiAuth (the dummy creds suggest UbiAuthentication may accept a guest login) + then the only live dependency is the RV/GATOR session service -> focus a fake/redirect on JUST GATOR.
(D) In-game bridge at the SOCKET layer: the RV stack is statically linked (BerkeleySocketDriver, 28au); intercept/redirect its sockets to a local relay or a fake RV/GATOR server so the RV op-cascade completes against us instead of Ubisoft.
Both are multi-day reverse/emulation efforts, not hooks. Also note our current hk_join short-circuits join_handler (returns 1, builds NO op) so no session forms at all - correct for probing, wrong for shipping.

Recorded for future; NOT building A/D now. Recommend confirming with Bantila which layer his "1.05 LAN" actually operates at before choosing A vs D. Deploy unchanged; launcher currently probe mode (should revert to safe).




### Session 28bd - RV guest-login probe LIVE result: co-op Host DOES send the RV/UbiAuth guest login and the async callback FIRES -> the emulation boundary is the RV/GATOR session service (route A), not the socket layer (D)

Built+ran the 28bc login probe (FC3COOP_PROBE_LOGIN=1; host/join short-circuits skipped so the real co-op op runs; internet ON). Owner navigated Co-op -> Host. coop_shim.log sequence:
- `gate -> success (LAN 127.0.0.1:9000)` (gate hook passed),
- **`RVLOGIN-SEND this=...`** = FUN_11246b90 executed -> the RV guest login (dummy creds UbiDummyPwd) was actually SENT, i.e. the op got past the 0x80030064/0x80030065 pre-checks and issued a real network login,
- **`RVLOGIN-CB this=...`** = FUN_11253890 (the async login-result callback) FIRED -> a RESPONSE came back (the login round-tripped over the network).
Then the game crashed (process gone) AFTER the callback.

Interpretation (A vs D discriminator, answered): the campaign co-op Host path genuinely performs a RendezVous/UbiAuthentication login exchange with the service and receives a callback -> the flow reaches and talks to the UbiAuth/RV network layer. So the right emulation target is the **RV/GATOR session service** (route A), not full socket-layer emulation (D). The crash is AFTER the callback, i.e. downstream (deeper RV session ops and/or our other hooks ticket_*/gate), NOT the login itself.
Not yet known: whether the callback reported ACCEPT (status>=0 -> proceed to RV session ops, which is consistent with the downstream crash) or REJECT (status<0 -> 0x8001000b error, no crash). The crash (not a clean error) is weak evidence the login PROCEEDED past reject (accept), but must be confirmed.

NEXT (refine, not yet done): (1) capture the RVLOGIN status = the return of FUN_11210df0 inside the callback (log eax scoped to the CB), to get accept-vs-reject definitively; (2) isolate the crash: run the probe with the other hooks (ticket_validate/ticket_prepare) DISABLED to see if the post-callback crash is our hook interference vs the game's own RV session op. Both are small shim changes.

Probe build = coop_shim.dll 134656B (src/coop_shim/coop_shim.cpp) with env FC3COOP_PROBE_LOGIN + patch_entry_n (6-byte entry displacement). Deployed to TestFC3_105/bin; launcher currently PROBE_LOGIN mode. Game not running. Internal Ubisoft addresses stay LOCAL.




### Session 28be - Refined login probe: RVLOGIN-SEND+CB fire but RVLOGIN-STATUS never reached; crash persists with ticket hooks DISABLED -> the crash is the game's own deep RV/GATOR session handling, NOT our hooks. Re-ran with FC3COOP_PROBE_LOGIN=1 + FC3COOP_NO_TICKET=1 (skip ticket_validate/ticket_prepare) + host/join short-circuits skipped + internet ON. Owner: Co-op -> Host.
