- The game STILL crashed with ticket hooks DISABLED -> our ticket_* hooks are exonerated; the crash is downstream in the game's real RV/GATOR session ops (or in online_flag/gate/rv_connected fakes putting the op in an inconsistent state that dies deeper).
Accept-vs-reject status not captured (guards skipped the call). Confirmed facts stand: (a) co-op Host performs a real RV/UbiAuth login round-trip (route A boundary confirmed, 28bd); (b) running the real RV session flow far enough to host a session CRASHES with hook-level patching regardless of ticket hooks.

CONCLUSION (strategic): hook-level / static patching cannot get past the UbiAuth/RV + GATOR session-establishment cascade. The only route that can work is to make the game talk to a LOCAL FAKE RendezVous/GATOR session service (route A) and satisfy the full op cascade's result-events + storm::echo session bringup. That is a multi-week protocol RE+build (RendezVous PRUDP application layer), matching the hub's hardest category. No cheap lever remains in this codebase (all levers now tried: gate fakes, provider swap, fake manager, online return-code fakes, force-LAN -- each falsified live).

### Session 28bg - Prep for the dev-session route: applied Bantila's party-loop + single-instance-mutex patches to our 1.06 working copy, created the dev-launch script, and confirmed the GATOR-fallback PRUDP protocol is the well-documented Quazal Rendez-Vous (de-risks the fallback)

**(a) PRUDP reference check (fallback insurance, web).** FC3's RendezVous is **Quazal Rendez-Vous (QRV)** ("seen in many many games, especially Ubisoft"); **RV clients use the original Rendez-Vous variation of PRUDP** (not Nintendo's PRUDPv0/v1). Public references exist and document the packet format (fields: src, dst, flags, type, sessionId, packetSignature, sequenceId, fragmentIndex, payload; little-endian) plus RV packet signature handling: NintendoClients Rendez-Vous wiki, `Stary2001/nex-dissector`, `PretendoNetwork/nex-go`. So if we ever fall back to the GATOR emulator (docs/coop-gator-emulation-plan.md), stage S1 (PRUDP codec) is **more tractable than feared** - the framing + signature of the RV PRUDP variant are publicly documented; the in-game session-service semantics remain the hard part. Not used on the primary route.

**(b) Working-copy patches (write to H:\FarCryCoop\TestFC3_105\bin, NOT the install; .orig backups kept).** Ran local/scratch/apply_bantila_patch.py (reuses scripts/patch_dll.py finders + patch):
- FC3.dll: party-loop `jbe->jmp` @0x10622c7a, mutex `jnz->6 NOP` @0x100041bb (7 bytes changed). Backup `FC3.dll.orig`.
- FC3_d3d11.dll: party-loop @0x1077e1df, mutex @0x10004092 (7 bytes). Backup `FC3_d3d11.dll.orig`.
- end-session NOP confirmed already present (0 call sites, from solo262 loader) -> patch_dll's end-session step is a no-op here.
These patches are benign for normal single-player / normal play (party-loop + end-session affect co-op only; mutex removal just allows a 2nd instance). To revert: copy the `.orig` files back over the DLLs.

**(c) Dev-launch script** `H:\FarCryCoop\TestFC3_105\dev_coop.ps1` (English comments; not committed, has local paths): `-Role host` runs `farcry3_d3d11.exe -host coop_vidar -login Host -coop` (windowed, `-skipintro -quickstartup`); `-Role client [-Ip <ipv4>]` runs `-client -login Guest -ip <Ip> -coop` and auto-detects a non-loopback IPv4 if -Ip omitted. **Does NOT set FC3COOP_SHIM**, so the coop shim loads inert (`coop_shim.cpp:524` returns before install_hooks when enable=0) and does not interfere with the dev session. The proxy loader still loads (the exe imports it) but is transparent.

**Recipe to test (owner's hands; 2 windows + UI observation):** host binds UDP 9000 on its FIRST adapter, client 9001; the client -Ip must be that adapter's IPv4 (loopback may not be it - on Bantila's PC the first adapter was a VirtualBox 192.168.56.1). Expected per Bantila: host loads coop_vidar, client joins, then possibly both freeze on an empty dark panel. If it reproduces, the next step is RE of the main-thread freeze (Bantila sample addrs 0x10338ad4 / 0x102f1b1d / 0x103e07db / 0x1040aaa3 / 0x10327bbd) to find what the co-op lobby waits on. **Open risk:** Ubisoft Connect DRM may refuse a direct `farcry3_d3d11.exe` launch (Bantila was on Steam 1.05); first run will show whether the engine even starts standalone.

### Session 28bl - dev-switch (-host/-client) route TESTED LIVE on the working mod-262 build and found DEAD. The 2-instance mutex patch WORKS. Frontier confirmed again = RV/GATOR application-layer session establishment.

Confirmed the working co-op install runs the exact RE-mapped binary: FC3_d3d11.dll MD5 46BCC0975905088D1FA4DF94B57B703D (1.05 FC3 binaries on a 1.06 install, see docs/fc3-coop-mod-setup.md), so every address in NOTES/PLAN is valid for it.

Live results (owner drives UI, we launch detached + watch netstat/WMP):
- Single-instance mutex NOP (FC3.dll 0x100041bb / FC3_d3d11.dll 0x10004092; via local/scratch/apply_bantila_patch.py) WORKS: two farcry3_d3d11.exe run at once (host bound UDP 9000 on the LAN adapter, client bound UDP 9001), both alive, no boot crash. Pristine mod-262 backups kept as *.dll.orig; our 2-instance build kept as *.dll.patched.
- `-host coop_vidar -login host1 -coop`: the process stays alive and binds UDP 9000 (a plain no-switch direct launch of farcry3_d3d11.exe exits immediately), so the switch IS consumed - BUT the game just sits on the main menu; it does NOT create a hosted session/lobby. Starting the co-op game from the menu afterwards CRASHES with the known signature farcry3_d3d11.exe / MSVCR100.dll / 0xc0000005 / offset 0x26df (identical to 28bj). => `-host` only spins up the Storm socket, it does not host.
- `-client -login client1 -coop -ip 192.168.1.8`: binds UDP 9001, no boot crash, but there is no joinable session to attach to (Storm sockets are unconnected `*:*`, so no client<->host association is visible in netstat).
- `-offline` (the 1.05 debug main menu with "COOP Story"/"Adversarial"/"UPlay", FUN_10922ac6 in Phase 1) does NOT appear on this retail 1.05 binary - just the normal menu. The debug menu is compiled out / gated in retail.

WHY multi-human cannot work in any offline path here: mod 262's menu entry "Offline coop" is the stock "Local Coop" playlist (sessionType 3) - a LOCAL/console-style session with NO network server, so a remote/LAN player has nothing to connect to (this is exactly why mod 262 is solo-only and why nobody has got 2 humans in via it). The only path that carries a real networked session is the Storm session created by the RV/GATOR front-end, and that front-end (the Ubisoft online service) is gone. The dev switches were supposed to reach that Storm session but on this retail build `-host` does not actually build the session object (menu-only / crash).

Conclusion / frontier unchanged: getting 2+ humans into a networked co-op needs APPLICATION-LAYER session establishment. Options, re-ranked now that we can patch THIS binary and can run 2 instances:
- A1 (now most attractive): stub/replace the game's `_RdV` provider answers IN-PROCESS (in the 1.05 FC3 DLL we already patch) so a "host" builds a local networked session (fake successful login/ticket/matchmaking -> local session id) and a "client" resolves the peer by address, then storm::echo carries the gameplay over UDP 9000/9001. We have the address map and the patcher; the work is RE of the `_RdV` provider vtables (login/matchmaking/invite + the session-create + address-resolve paths) to see how little must be faked. Kill-switch unknown: whether an in-process stub is enough to enter a "hosted, accepting-joins, networked" state.
- A2: a local RendezVous/GATOR PRUDP emulator (see docs/coop-gator-emulation-plan.md), ~13-24 weeks, two kill-switch unknowns (prudps crypto, storm::echo peer mapping).
- B: our own netcode on top of the local "Offline coop" session - hardest (would bypass the engine's session layer entirely).

Next concrete step (no game needed): static RE of the 1.05 `_RdV` session-provider vtables (login op, matchmaking op, the function that creates/joins the Storm session and reads the peer address) to scope A1. Reversible state: TestFC3_105 bin restored to pristine mod 262 (MD5 46BCC097); 2-instance patch preserved as *.dll.patched.

WHAT THE DEV SWITCHES ACTUALLY DO (host FUN_106518ba, client FUN_10651d10):
- Both are CONFIG POPULATORS, not session creators. They parse the command line into a session-launch config object (param: a struct; iVar3/iVar1). Host: -login required; -host(map) -bfname -dedicated -sessionuid; game mode from defaults DAT_117097d4..9804; sets config byte [*(cfg+0xa0)+0xd]=1, [cfg+0x64]=FUN_105b91b3(), [cfg+0x40]=sessionuid(u64 via _strtoui64), [cfg+0xb8]=ranked, dedicated -> FUN_104c0d5a; session TYPE via FUN_104c0ca4(3 local / 2 with -online); flags FUN_104c0d1b(1)/FUN_104c0cfa(1). Both are called ONLY from FUN_10652061 (the cmdline dispatcher).
- CONCLUSION for the live "just menu" result: `-host` only PRESETS a launch config; it does not itself call any "create Storm session now" function (the client path is the one that calls FUN_1065296f to start a connect). So on retail the host config is not executed into a live session unless something downstream consumes it (a menu action / the RV front-end) -> host sits at menu. This matches 28bl exactly.

DIRECT-IP JOIN EXISTS (the hopeful part for A1): the client has `-ip <address>` + `SearchIdAddress` in the join-param struct (FUN_11342660/FUN_11342740: SearchIdAddress+0x8, SearchSessionId+0x10, JoinSessionId+0x18, SessionRefId+0x20, PlaylistId+0x34, SessionType+0x38, NetEngineType+0x3c) and session ops CSessionSearchIPOperation / CFCXJoinSessionOperation. There is a special localhost branch. So the engine can attempt a session join BY ADDRESS, not only via RV matchmaking search. Kill-switch unknown stays: does the IP-join still require an RV-issued ticket/SessionRefId, or is the IP enough to complete a storm::echo handshake directly.

_RdV LOGIN PROVIDER (FUN_110cd961 ctor/config, FUN_110cd71d tick/execute, FUN_110ccb46) is a RendezVous job STATE MACHINE: it runs RV jobs via thunk_FUN_110a2ceb (job runner), FUN_110b81a3 (submit), FUN_10288b35 (wait/lock), FUN_110ccc47 (create job), and returns codes into the provider object (e.g. [obj+0xc]=0x1a on a failure branch; flags at obj+0x40/0x44/0x54/0x60). IMPORTANT: this is the account/login front-end, which mod 262's fake-Uplay ALREADY effectively neutralizes for local play (the game reaches the "Offline coop" local menu = login/account step satisfied). So faking these methods is NOT the main A1 cost; the cost is the session create/advertise + client IP-join completion.

1. Host side: force the host launch config to execute into a real Storm session (find + call CFCXCreateSessionOperation / the session-create op with the config; make the host bind 9000 and ADVERTISE). Today `-host` presets but does not execute (see above).
2. Client side: drive CFCXJoinSessionOperation via SearchIdAddress = host IP so storm::echo connects directly (the localhost branch is a template for a direct path).
3. Login: already satisfied by mod 262 fake-Uplay; at most stub the RV login provider methods to report "already logged in / success" so the app doesn't block on the RV job.

### Session 28bn - A1 scope continued (static RE on FC3_105_OW, FC3.dll 1.05): the session layer is a named-operation registry and the LAN primitives are ALREADY IN THE ENGINE. Verdict: A1 is viable and cheaper than feared; ONE kill-switch left to verify.

FUN_1065296f (the "tail call" of the client handler) is just a string-cleanup wrapper (FUN_10001cd2 assign + FUN_100057a7 free) - NOT a start-connect. OPERATION REGISTRY (found via the plain class-name strings; script DecompileA1e.java/run_a1e.py, output a1e_decompile.txt). Each session op is registered by name via FUN_11026001("<Name>", <factory/instance>) and its class singleton is a DAT pointer:
- CFCXCreateSessionOperation -> singleton &DAT_1184a118 (reg func FUN_10684e4b; factory FUN_10658db2)
- CFCXJoinSessionOperation  -> singleton &DAT_11853e20 (reg func FUN_107d48e0; factory FUN_10658db2)

- CSessionCreateOperation   -> &DAT_11eaf308 (reg func FUN_110b127d; factory FUN_108544d6)
- CSessionJoinOperation     -> &DAT_11eaf380 (reg func FUN_110b1359; factory FUN_108544d6)
- CSessionSearchIPOperation -> &DAT_11eaf368 (reg func FUN_110b132d; factory FUN_108544d6)
=> the game runs CFCX* (FC3-level) ops which wrap the lower CSession* ops; a join-by-IP op (CSessionSearchIPOperation) EXISTS as a first-class named op.

LAN PRIMITIVES ALREADY IN-ENGINE (this is the hopeful discovery): FUN_106516f1 (called by BOTH host and client handlers) parses up to FOUR accounts -login/-login2/-login3/-login4 (+ -password..-password4, default online-key string "a123456"), and for each writes an account-table entry setting field +0x18 = 2. Per our own account-flag map (NOTES: Offline=1, LAN=2, Live=4) that is accountFlags = **LAN**. So the dev-launch path itself assigns LAN accounts and reserves 4 player slots; combined with the client `-ip` (+ localhost branch) and CSessionSearchIPOperation, direct LAN join is a designed-in engine capability that the retail UI just never routes to.

A1 VERDICT: the heavy part (faking RV login) is unnecessary - mod 262 fake-Uplay already satisfies account/login, and the LAN account flags + IP-search op are present. Real A1 work = make the host actually EXECUTE CFCXCreateSessionOperation from its launch record (bind 9000, advertise) and drive the client through CFCXJoinSessionOperation with SearchIdAddress = host IP, so storm::echo carries gameplay.

SINGLE REMAINING KILL-SWITCH (decides "cheap A1" vs "must do A2/PRUDP"): does CFCXJoinSessionOperation (&DAT_11853e20 vtable) complete with only SearchIdAddress, or does it MANDATE a SessionRefId / ticket that only the RV service issues? Answer it by either (a) next RE step: read the vtable at &DAT_11853e20 (and &DAT_1184a118 for create) and decompile the execute slots, looking for RV/ticket calls; or (b) a live experiment once we can force a host session to exist. If join needs no RV ticket -> A1 is a bounded in-DLL patch (execute existing ops + fake success). If it needs a ticket -> A2 (RendezVous/PRUDP emulator) or the CFCX-layer ticket stub is unavoidable.

### Session 28bo - Static vtable read of the session ops is a DEAD END: the ops are dispatched by a name-based runtime reflection registry, so their execute methods are NOT statically reachable from the class singletons. The ticket-vs-IP question must be answered by the join-param field-writer or, better, EMPIRICALLY (dedicated-server live test).

Evidence (scripts DecompileA1g.java/DecompileA1d.java, outputs a1g/a1d_decompile.txt):
- The op class singletons &DAT_11853e20 (CFCXJoinSessionOperation) and &DAT_1184a118 (CFCXCreateSessionOperation) are ZERO-FILLED .bss (all dwords 0 in the file). Their vtables are installed at runtime, so there is no static vtable to walk.
- The "factories" FUN_10658db2 / FUN_108544d6 are themselves registration wrappers: FUN_10658db2 registers base class string "CFCXGameOperation" (via FUN_11025fe9 with instance from FUN_1039c752); FUN_108544d6 registers "CSessionOperation" (FUN_11025fe9 + FUN_1024bd70). And 28bn's FUN_11026001 registered the concrete op by name. So the op system is Ubisoft script-reflection: RegisterClass(name, base) / RegisterObject(name, ctor); instances and vtables are built at runtime, dispatch is by name string.

CONSEQUENCE: cannot statically prove whether CFCXJoinSessionOperation needs an RV ticket by reading a vtable slot. Two ways forward:
- (RE, slower) find the code that FILLS the join-param struct (fields registered by FUN_11342660/FUN_11342740: SearchIdAddress+0x8, SearchSessionId+0x10, JoinSessionId+0x18, SessionRefId+0x20, SessionType+0x38, NetEngineType+0x3c) and see whether a ticket/SessionRefId fetch (RV) precedes running the join op, or only SearchIdAddress (IP) is set.
- (EMPIRICAL, decisive & preferred) the host already binds UDP 9000 and the host handler supports -dedicated (FUN_104c0d5a). A DEDICATED server (-dedicated -host coop_vidar -login host1 -coop, session type 3) should create a headless session and ADVERTISE/listen WITHOUT the RV menu path, and a second instance `-client -login c -coop -ip 127.0.0.1` then joins via storm::echo. If the dedicated host stays alive with 9000 and the client connects (UDP assoc / packet exchange) -> join needs no RV ticket -> A1 is a bounded in-DLL patch. If the client can bind 9001 but never reaches the dedicated host, or the host dies, -> the join path is gated on RV (A2/PRUDP). This is also the first real multi-human test, the actual goal.

State: no game files changed (pure static RE). All A1 RE artifacts in scratch (a1c/a1d/a1e/a1g_decompile.txt) + scripts DecompileA1c..g.java + run_a1c..g.py.




### Session 28bp - DECISIVE EMPIRICAL TEST of the dev-launch / dedicated path: a JOINABLE NETWORKED co-op session on PC cannot start without the Ubisoft online/RV service. This closes the "cheap A1" hope and points to A2 (local RendezVous/GATOR emulator) as the realistic path. Ran on the working build with the 2-instance (.patched) DLLs, then restored pristine (MD5 46BCC097).

Results (owner read the screens; we launched detached + watched netstat/WMP):
- 2 instances coexist (mutex patch) and no crash - confirmed again.
- `-dedicated -host coop_vidar -login host1 -coop` (session TYPE 3, local): the dedicated host STAYS ALIVE and binds UDP 9000 on 127.0.0.1 (loopback). But it does NOT create a joinable session: launching `-client -login client1 -coop -ip 127.0.0.1` gives two processes that BOTH just sit on the MAIN MENU; the client never binds 9001 / never reaches a connect; no join. Type 3 is the LOCAL/console session -> nothing on the wire to join. (Matches 28bn: dev switches only fill a launch-record.)
- `-dedicated -host coop_vidar -login host1 -coop -online` (session TYPE 2, the NETWORKED kind a remote player could join): the process EXITS within ~5 s (procs=0, NO crash event). The online/networked session bootstrap aborts immediately because the Ubisoft online/RV service it requires at startup is gone.

CONCLUSION (empirical, strong): the ONLY session type that supports remote join (type 2 / online) cannot even START on this build now (instant exit, needs the online service); the only type that starts offline (type 3 / local) has no network to join. So no combination of stock dev switches yields a joinable co-op session. Bantila's frozen lobby and mod 262's solo-only are the same wall, now reproduced on our stable base.

IMPLICATION FOR THE CHOSEN ROUTE:
- A1 (stub the _RdV providers IN-PROCESS and run the existing ops) is NOT cheap after all: the type-2 online bootstrap aborts before any op runs, so we would first have to fake the online-service startup itself (make the game believe RV is reachable at init) before host/join ops could even execute - and the op layer is opaque script-reflection (28bo). Effectively A1 must recreate what the online service provides anyway.
- A2 (local RendezVous/GATOR PRUDP emulator; docs/coop-gator-emulation-plan.md) is the path that makes the game's OWN online flow boot and drive create/join: we redirect the RV service hosts to a local emulator that answers the login/session bootstrap, the game runs its normal "private co-op session" menu (type 2), and storm::echo carries gameplay. The game drives the ops (we don't fight the reflection layer); we only answer RV. Two kill-switches remain (PRUDP crypto, storm::echo peer mapping) but the architecture fits.
- B (own netcode over the local type-3 session) remains the hardest (replace the engine session layer).

Next concrete step for A2: inventory exactly which RV/online endpoints + handshake the game contacts during the (now-aborting) `-online` boot and during a menu "create private co-op" - i.e. capture the outbound UDP/PRUDP + HTTPS to onlineconfigservice.ubi.com / rendezvous hosts, so the local emulator's minimum surface is known. (Needs a packet capture during an `-online` launch / menu attempt.)

Key result: Ubisoft's OnlineConfig service is STILL ONLINE, but the FC3 session/RV host is DEAD. So A2 must emulate the RendezVous/session server; the config bootstrap may be reusable.

Static harvest (Python string scan of FC3.dll / FC3_d3d11.dll / uplay_r14.dll / ubiorbitapi loader):

Quazal is compiled into the FC3 binary: tokens `Rendezvous / RendezVous / GATOR / Matchmaking / STUN / punchtraversal / NATTraversal / Passport / orbitapi`. => the RV (Quazal RendezVous) client lives in FC3.dll itself, not in the Ubisoft Connect libs (uplay_r14.dll is the modded Uplay stub referencing uplay_r1.dll).

- **`fc3-bloomberg-server.ubisoft.org` NXDOMAIN** (dead). This is the FC3 session/RV backend.
- ubi.com resolves (AWS 18.210.19.179 etc.); ubisoft.org has an empty A record.

WHY `-dedicated -host ... -online` exits in ~1 s (28bp) - refined hypothesis: the game reaches OnlineConfig (up), obtains the session/RV endpoint (which points at the now-dead bloomberg host / an RV service), and the RV connect fails instantly (NXDOMAIN -> immediate abort), so the type-2 session bootstrap dies before opening a game socket (consistent with zero sockets/DNS seen in 28bp's tight poll). NOT yet confirmed which exact host the co-op path dials (needs the menu private-coop path, where the game stays alive, to capture the live RV host).

A2 SCOPE (concrete):

Two sub-options: (a) hosts-file redirect `fc3-bloomberg-server.ubisoft.org` (and any other dead RV host the menu path reveals) to 127.0.0.1 and run a local Quazal RendezVous emulator there; (b) if config itself must be intercepted, emulate OnlineConfigService.svc (SOAP) too.

### Session 28br - Live menu-path network capture pins the FIRST gate of the online path: FC3 calls gconnect.ubi.com over PLAIN HTTP/80 and the host is ALIVE but returns a "service unavailable" state (server-side kill switch), which is why the UI shows "Far Cry 3 service is not available. Please try again later." (loc id 145556 XLSP_DOWN). Emulation target = GATOR/gconnect, and layer 1 is plaintext HTTP (trivial to intercept - no TLS).

Owner launched the game normally (upc), opened Multiplayer->Co-op->private host; UI showed the error (screenshot). Our poller (by process name, no admin) captured for the whole attempt:
- TCP: the game connected **216.98.50.240:80 (HTTP, plaintext)** then state CloseWait (server answered and closed). reverse-DNS of 216.98.50.240 = **gconnect.ubi.com** (same IP as onlineconfigservice.ubi.com's LB). So the online status/login service is **gconnect.ubi.com** = Ubisoft **GATOR / Gconnect**, reachable, HTTP/80.
- UDP binds by the game: **0.0.0.0:3074** (the GATOR/console port) and **192.168.1.8:9000** (Storm OnlineEnginePort on the LAN adapter) - so the game opens the data-plane ports but the session never forms because the gconnect status/login returns "down" first.
- No new DNS cache entries (gconnect/onlineconfig were already cached), and **fc3-bloomberg-server.ubisoft.org was NOT resolved** -> the game aborts at the gconnect "service unavailable" check before ever dialing the RV/session backend. Confirms the order: gconnect status/login (HTTP) FIRST, and that gate fails with a server-returned "down".

KEY DE-RISK FOR A2:
- The first layer is **PLAIN HTTP on port 80** to gconnect.ubi.com (no TLS) -> a local logging HTTP server + a hosts-file redirect of gconnect.ubi.com (and the onlineconfig aliases) to 127.0.0.1 lets us capture the EXACT request(s) the game sends and replay/answer them to get past the "service unavailable" gate. This is the cheapest, highest-value next A2 step and needs no crypto.
- After that gate, the game will proceed to the RV/PRUDP (Quazal) layer and open UDP 3074/9000; those later layers still carry the known PRUDP-crypto kill-switch, but we can reach them once gconnect answers OK.

A2 CONCRETE NEXT STEP (needs owner admin for the hosts file, which is a C:\Windows system file): (1) append to hosts: 127.0.0.1 gconnect.ubi.com, onlineconfigservice.ubi.com, cert-/uat- aliases; (2) run a tiny local HTTP-80 logger (Python http.server subclass that logs method/path/headers/body and returns a placeholder 200) to record what the game requests when trying to go online; (3) relaunch + navigate online, then read the logged requests to learn the gconnect protocol the game expects. Keep the original hosts file backed up. The game also uses port 3074 UDP - note for later PRUDP work.

State: game was still running at capture end (owner can close). No game files changed by us this session. This is the strongest evidence yet that A2 (GATOR/gconnect emulator) is the viable path and that its first layer is plaintext HTTP (interceptable without TLS).




### Session 28bs - MAJOR UNLOCK for A2. (1) The live online-config kill switch is confirmed: GET GetOnlineConfig?onlineConfigID=21fc0b75f83c49f2a51726f60fd1c5f8&target=client now returns **HTTP 200 text/xml but ZERO bytes** (empty) - that empty config is exactly what makes FC3 show "service unavailable" (our logger returning empty 200 reproduced the same error). (2) The FC3 game's ONLY online request captured is that GetOnlineConfig GET (empty body, headers `Host: onlineconfigservice.ubi.com`, `Accept: */*`); it does NOT reach gconnect until it gets a non-empty config. (3) The config SCHEMA + the RV redirect seam are recovered from Wayback.

Config = a JSON array of {"Name":..,"Values":[..]}. Fields seen for other Ubisoft titles (Wayback, other onlineConfigIDs; FC3's id not archived):
- **SandboxUrl** = `prudp://address=<RV host>;port=<port>`  and **SandboxUrlWS** = `<RV host>:<port>`  -> THE Quazal RendezVous (RV) endpoint the game's PRUDP client dials (examples: pdc-vm-rdv05.ubisoft.com ports 23900 / 21010; mdc-mm-rdv21.ubisoft.com port 22250).
- **AuthenticationServer** = `lb-agora.ubisoft.com:3081` (Agora = Ubisoft auth). **LobbyServer** = `lb-lsg-prod.ubisoft.com:3105`.
- Client extras: MmpTitleId (e.g. 0xA004), uplay_* base URLs. Server-target config: genome_id, mmp_svc_key, mmp_title_id, pkws_url, product_id, uws_url (ubiaccountservice.ubi.com/legacy/AgoraAuthentication.asmx).

ARCHITECTURAL CONCLUSION (redirect seam, no crypto needed for the seam): because the config supplies the RV endpoint as `prudp://address=...;port=...`, we do NOT have to hosts-redirect the RV host - we just SERVE FC3 a crafted GetOnlineConfig whose SandboxUrl points at **127.0.0.1:<our port>**. Then FC3's own Quazal RV client connects to OUR local PRUDP server; we implement the Quazal RV handshake (PRUDP login + create/join session + peer routing) and storm::echo carries gameplay. The RV protocol is **Quazal PRUDP** (known middleware; public reverse-engineering exists), so this is tractable.

CONCRETE NEXT STEPS:
1. Serve FC3 a crafted GetOnlineConfig JSON via the logger (set GATOR_CANNED=<fc3_config.json>) - start with the fields above (SandboxUrl/SandboxUrlWS -> 127.0.0.1:<port>, AuthenticationServer, LobbyServer, MmpTitleId) and iterate: FC3 reads specific keys; add them until the "unavailable" error clears and the game tries to dial the PRUDP endpoint.
   - Need FC3's expected keys/ids: read them from FC3.dll (grep the config keys it parses: SandboxUrl, SandboxUrlWS, AuthenticationServer, LobbyServer, MmpTitleId, plus any FC3-specific names) OR iterate empirically.
2. Run a local UDP listener on 127.0.0.1:<port> to CAPTURE the first PRUDP packets FC3 sends once it trusts our config - the PRUDP header/version/login is the reverse target. (scripts/gator_mock/udp_sniffer.py.)
3. Then implement the Quazal RV server responses (there are public Quazal/RendezVous references).

Artifacts: live empty-config proof + Wayback schema (this section). Tools: scripts/gator_mock/{http_logger.py,hosts_patch.py,README}. State: game closed by owner; hosts may still be redirected - restore when idle. No game files changed.




### Session 28bt - FC3 online architecture fully mapped from FC3.dll strings + first live A2 capture results.

LIVE capture result (run_capture.py served fc3_config.json = non-empty, 212 bytes): the game fetched GetOnlineConfig once, then **made NO other HTTP request and never dialled the RV SandboxUrl (127.0.0.1:50000)** - netwatch showed the game resolving the LIVE modern UbiServices stack (public-ubiservices.ubi.com -> 3.173.11.203 AWS; public-ws-ubiservices.ubi.com -> 166.117.137.155/173) - those go through Ubisoft Connect (upc.exe), not the game PID - and the game UDP-bound 192.168.1.8:9000 (Storm) + :52212 (peer). So after config the game halts at the LOGIN step and never reaches RV session dial.

FC3.dll string map (the whole online core):
- **Login state machine** enum order: Invalid -> NotLoggedIn -> NotLoggedInAborted -> **FetchOnlineConfig -> FetchOnlineConfigRdy -> LoggingIn(Rdy) -> LoggingInFinalizing -> LoggedIn -> LoggedInUplayServices...** ; plus `CFCXGatorLoginCheckOperation` = the GATOR login check inside LoggingIn.
- **Everything online is the Quazal RendezVous (RV) provider set**: `CGame{Matchmaking,Friend,Location,QueueService,Messaging,Privilege,OnlineData,Intel,TitleStorage,UserStorage,Tracking,Invite,FrontendService}_Provider_RdV` and `CSession{Create,Join,Search,SearchSessionId,Start,End,Delete,Update,Login,Logout,FinalizeLogin,PlayerAdd/Remove,SetupConnectivityToUsers,SendMessage,MigrateHost,...}Operation_RdV` driven by `CSessionManagerImp_RdV`. Config switch `UseRdV` toggles it.
- **RV connection is ENCRYPTED PRUDPS**: URI template `prudps:/address=%s;port=%d;stream=%d;sid=%d;PID=%d;CID=1;type=%d` (and `prudp:/address=`) built in `JobBackEndServicesLoginWithData.cpp` which reads **`SandboxUrl`** from the online config. So SandboxUrl is consumed at BACK-END LOGIN (JobBackEndServicesLoginWithData), and the transport is PRUDPS (Quazal secure PRUDP) -> this is the crypto wall (PRUDP handshake + RC4/signature).
- **GATOR is the RV backend's service name**: a full error enum `E_SESSION_ERROR_GATOR_*` includes `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, `INVALID_TICKET/EXPIRED_TICKET/INVALID_TOKEN/INVALID_AUTH_TOKEN/COULD_NOT_RETRIEVE_NP_TICKET`, `CRYPT_FAILURE`, `NOT_SIGNED_IN`, `NO_PRIVILEGE`, `HTTP_*` -> login needs a valid Uplay NP ticket/token; `CFCXGatorLoginCheckOperation` + `GATOR_NOT_SIGNED_IN/COULD_NOT_RETRIEVE_NP_TICKET` is the likely abort point (no ticket -> stop before RV).
- **`fc3-bloomberg-server.ubisoft.org` is NOT the session host** - it is telemetry/crash (`CBloombergConfig`, `bloomberg.reporter.crash.exe`, `bloomberg.listener.crash.exe`, `-bloombergstats`). The RV host is only `SandboxUrl` (not present in the dead bloomberg path).
- **LAN-matchmaking shortcut strings** (storm::echo net-engine startup params table, with human descriptions): `LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable`, `LanMatchmakingReuseAddress`, `ForceRouting`, `DisableRouting`, `DetectUploadBandwidth`, alongside `EchoStartupParamsWin32`, `MatchMakingEnabled`, `MaxMatchmakingTime`, `MaxJoinAttempts`, `PingPeriod`, `SearchPeriod`, `Weight*`. These are engine net settings (not the online config); enabling loopback LAN discovery may let sessions be discovered/joined locally. There is also a `-sandbox` cmdline switch and a `Software\Quazal` registry key, and `MatchMakingEnabled`.

CONCLUSION: A2 needs TWO things in order: (1) satisfy the GATOR login check (needs a valid Ubisoft NP ticket/token - probably obtainable via a running, logged-in Ubisoft Connect since UbiServices is LIVE, OR forge one), (2) implement the Quazal **PRUDPS** RV server (the crypto wall) and the CSession*Operation_RdV create/join flow. The cheapest possible shortcut is the storm::echo **LAN-matchmaking-over-loopback** settings + LAN account flags (28bn), which may bypass RV/GATOR entirely for a LAN-style 2-4 player session - worth an experiment.

NEXT EXPERIMENTS:
1. Confirm current in-game behaviour with the served config (did "service unavailable" change to a sign-in error?). If sign-in error -> ticket/token is the wall; check whether launching through a logged-in Ubisoft Connect gives FC3 a ticket.
2. Look for FC3's own online log (it writes to %USERPROFILE%\Documents\My Games\Far Cry 3 on C: - needs owner consent to read) to capture the exact E_SESSION_ERROR_GATOR_* code at the abort.
3. Storm::echo LAN-loopback experiment: find where EchoStartupParamsWin32 / LanMatchmaking* are loaded (config file or registry) and try LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable=1 + LanMatchmakingReuseAddress=1 + MatchMakingEnabled=1 with a local host, no RV.

No online/gator log file is written (only GamerProfile.xml + TutorialProfile.xml). The **`GamerProfile.xml` `<NetworkProfile>`** is the engine's parsed net config and contains the exact keys found in FC3.dll as a settings schema (with human descriptions in-binary): `OnlineEnginePort=9000`, `OnlineServicePort=9001`, `FileTransferHostPort/ClientPort=9002/9003`, **`LanHostBroadcastPort=9004`**, **`LanClientBroadcastPort=9005`**, `ScanFreePorts=1`, `ScanPortStart=9000`, `ScanPortRange=1000`, `CustomMapMaxUploadRateInBitsOnline/Lan`, **`SessionProvider=""` (EMPTY)**, and `<Accounts />` (child element `Account`).

SessionProvider="Lan">`; launch host (create co-op) + a second instance (patched 2-instance DLL) to join via LAN discovery. If "Lan" is rejected/ignored, read the provider-string parser xref (SessionProvider 0x14ee1b8 -> factory map) for the exact accepted value, or populate `<Accounts><Account .../></Accounts>`.

### Session 28bw - LAN-provider shortcut is a DEAD END (re-confirmed) + root cause of the 28bv confusion + full launch-switch inventory.

Why SessionProvider=Lan removed the boot/login error but co-op still failed: the PC game layer has NO LAN session provider. NOTES 2026-10-25 (line ~163) already proved every game-side online provider exists only as `_RdV` (Ubisoft RendezVous), `_UplayPC` or `_Win`; Storm's `storm::echo::lan` exists below the game layer but the FCX game code never instantiates it. The `Online` / `Lan` tokens at 0x1560650/0x1560658 are Storm-echo session-TYPE state names (IdleState/SearchState/JoinState/CreateState/SessionState/MergeState + Online/Lan/Error), not a selectable FCX provider. So `SessionProvider="Lan"` matches no registered provider factory -> it just SKIPS online provider init (hence no login/boot error) while the co-op menu (which needs a real provider) still fails. The 28bv "boot error gone" was this skip, not progress toward co-op.

Why the boot error came back in 28bv's follow-up: hosts is `applied` again (gconnect/onlineconfig -> 127.0.0.1). The front-end "Connect to Far Cry 3 service" gate is checked even in Lan mode, and a broken 127.0.0.1 redirect (no proper answer) makes it fail. When hosts was restored (real Ubisoft Connect reaches the LIVE ubiservices) the front-end passed. CONCLUSION: keep hosts RESTORED for normal play; the gator_mock redirect is only for online-path capture experiments, not for playing. The RV UDP:50000 listener received nothing again (co-op never reaches RV).

Full launch-switch inventory from FC3.dll (281 `-word` tokens; online/session-relevant): `-coop`, `-host`, `-client` (used in dev scripts), `-dedicated`, **`-ip`** (direct-IP join), **`-sessionuid`**, `-login` / `-login2` / `-login3` / `-login4`, `-quickmatch`, `-offline`, `-online`, `-sandbox`, `-forcevip`, `-keyonline`, `-map`, `-language`, `-skipintro`, and **`-splitscreen` / `-splitscreen4`** (split-screen co-op on ONE machine).

The account-flag map (NOTES 2026-10-25 line 161) is definitive: `Offline`=1, `LAN`=2, `Live`=4, `Gator`=0x20, `Rendezvous`=0x40. `<Accounts><Account login="Host" accountFlags="2"/></Accounts>` was already tried (menus unchanged). We reverted GamerProfile.xml SessionProvider to the default empty value so the owner's working mod-262 solo-offline-coop state is preserved.

STRATEGIC STATE (unchanged from 28bp, now reinforced): the offline co-op session runs locally (Offline account flags, no provider) - that is what mod 262 gives (1 human + offline slot). A SECOND HUMAN needs a session provider, and the only ones the game knows (`_RdV`) talk to dead Ubisoft RV. So the real choices are: (A1/A2) become or emulate the `_RdV` provider (Quazal RV PRUDP - crypto wall), or (B) run our own netcode on top of the working Offline co-op session and inject a remote human as a player. ONE NEW CHEAP OPTION surfaced by the switch inventory: **`-splitscreen` / `-splitscreen4`** could give 2-4 humans on a SINGLE PC (split-screen co-op) with NO network at all - untested on this build, worth a quick owner test as a same-PC multi-human fallback.

### Session 28bz - A2 feasibility ASSESSMENT (Quazal RendezVous / PRUDP emulation for the stock Co-op menu). Evidence-based, from FC3.dll crypto artifacts + public PRUDP state.

CRYPTO STACK in FC3.dll: **OpenSSL 0.9.8l** (RC4 present: `RC4 part of OpenSSL 0.9.8l`; MD5/SHA1/SHA-256; RSA-SHA1 sigs) + **zlib 1.2.5**. A `blowfish` string also present. This matches the public Quazal PRUDP scheme exactly: after a small cleartext connection handshake, packet payloads are **zlib-compressed + RC4-encrypted** with a key derived at connect.

KEY-MODEL EVIDENCE (decisive for feasibility): the client references **`GameSessionKey`** (150+ refs) and errors `E_SESSION_ERROR_RENDEZVOUS_GAMESESSION_INVALIDSESSIONKEY` and `E_SESSION_ERROR_GATOR_KEY_DECRYPTION_FAILED` -> the client RECEIVES a GameSessionKey from the RV server and DECRYPTS it locally. In Quazal RendezVous this is the standard model where the per-title RV/GameSessionKey material is **embedded in the client** (so any legit client can decrypt what the RV issues). Because we HAVE the binary, that key/derivation is **extractable** - this is the single most important de-risk and it looks achievable.

TICKET MODEL: session tickets are minted by the RV/Storm flow itself - `storm::echo::SessionTicketRequestAttachment`, `SessionTicketAttachment`, `inEvent::RequestTicketSessionRefId`, `UseTicketRequestAttachment`, `SessionTicketTimeoutInMs` -> the RV server (us) issues the session ticket; it is NOT a Ubisoft-signed DRM blob. The `NP_TICKET` / `NOT_SIGNED_IN_NP` strings are console/platform entitlement branches, not the PC RV path. So no Ubisoft private key is required to satisfy the session-ticket step.

PUBLIC PROTOCOL STATE: Quazal PRUDP is well reverse-engineered - Hackaday "Analysing PRUDP protocol", wiki/Grokipedia/gist writeups, and importantly an **open-source Python PRUDP server emulator** (GitHub PR by 0xZontier, "PRUDP: Python-based server emulator for Nintendo DSi"), plus community PRUDP server libraries (Nintendo netplay). PRUDP packet format (magic byte, header signature/CRC, conn/session IDs, message/sequence ids, reliability flags), connection handshake (Connection Request/Reply/Ping/Ack) and RC4+zlib payload are documented. FC3's RV = the same Quazal lineage.

HONEST COST/RISK SPLIT for A2:
- PRUDP transport + handshake + RC4/zlib: LOW risk (public + open implementations to port).
- Extract the embedded RV/GameSessionKey + key derivation from FC3.dll: MEDIUM (find the constant + the connect-time KDF; RC4 is already in the binary so the scheme matches public docs).
- Reconstruct the Quazal RendezVous SERVICE-layer messages (login, session create/search/join, ticket issuance) that FC3's `CSession*Operation_RdV` expect: MEDIUM-HIGH - the real work. Not published for FC3 specifically, but tractable because we can read the RV client code AND, once a PRUDP socket is up, capture the client's request/response messages black-box.
- No confirmed Ubisoft-side signature wall on the session/ticket path (RV issues them); front-end "connected" state is satisfiable (in Lan mode the game already booted online-gate-free).

VERDICT: A2 is FEASIBLE but is a multi-week RE+emulation effort (weeks, not days), gated on (1) extracting the embedded RV key, (2) reconstructing the RV service messages. The big payoff: the game's OWN battle-tested co-op netcode (sessions, sync, host migration) then does all the hard work for free, and peers join exactly like the stock menu - the cleanest route to the original "friends without port-forwarding" goal (we front it with our relay).

RECOMMENDED CHEAP DE-RISK before committing weeks: one Ghidra spike to (a) confirm PRUDP header handling (magic byte/CRC) in the storm::echo connect code, and (b) locate the embedded RV/GameSessionKey constant + its connect-time KDF. If the key is a clean static blob, A2 becomes clearly the preferred path over Plan B (B fights the engine's assumption that remote players require a provider).




### Session 28ca - A2 Ghidra SPIKE (pass 1+2): PRUDP/RV machinery confirmed present; embedded-key hunt NOT yet resolved; key ordering insight.

Ran two headless Ghidra scripts (scripts/ghidra/DecompileA2.java, DecompileA2b.java) against the FC3_105_OW project (FC3.dll 1.05, base 0x10000000), string-anchored decompilation.
CONFIRMED (good for A2): the full Quazal RV / storm::echo client is compiled in - PRUDP type strings (19x "PRUDP"), URI builders `prudp:/address=`, `prudps:/address=%s;port=%d;stream=%d;...` (VAs 0x11670ec8/0x11671dc8), `stream=` parsers, `GameSessionKey` (83x) + `m_sessionKey`, `storm::echo::SessionTicketRequestAttachment`, `inEvent::RequestTicketSessionRefId`, and the RV session error enum (FUN_110bbd4c = string resolver) enumerating the exact contract our server must satisfy: MUST_LOGIN_FIRST, LOGIN_FAILED, ALREADY_LOGGED_IN, TICKET_REQUEST_FAILED, CREATE_SESSION_EXISTS/NO_LOCAL_CLIENT, JOIN_FAILED(_VERSION_MISMATCHED/_SESSION_NOT_FOUND/_SESSION_FULL/_SESSION_ALREADY_IN/_NO_PING_RESPONSE/_ARBITRATED_GAME_IN_PROGRESS), SEARCH_TIMEOUT, PING, etc.
NOT RESOLVED: the embedded PRUDP/GameSessionKey constant + connect-time KDF did NOT surface via string cross-references (the format-string references are indirect/table-loaded; the 4 functions directly referencing the key strings are small accessors/serializers with no crypto calls). Locating the key needs a deeper targeted hunt (find the RV packet-framing/connect-send function by its socket send in the RV module ~0x116xxxxx, then its key/KDF), or runtime capture.
KEY ORDERING INSIGHT (raises A2 difficulty, honest): the client emits the PRUDP Connection Request to SandboxUrl only when it reaches `JobBackEndServicesLoginWithData` - i.e. the `_RdV` provider must RUN. In Lan mode the provider is skipped (no connect); in online mode the front-end login gate (Uplay token) fails first. So we CANNOT "just run it and sniff PRUDP" against a fake RV until the `_RdV` provider is forced to connect - which is what the `coop_shim` `FC3COOP_HOOK_PROVIDER` path (NOTES 28as) was meant to do. Therefore the practical A2 de-risk is: use coop_shim to FORCE the RV provider to connect to our fake SandboxUrl (127.0.0.1:50000) + run the udp_sniffer, capture the cleartext PRUDP Connection Request, then match it against the static RV connect function to extract the header format + key.
Net: A2 machinery + message contract are real and mapped; the crypto/key extraction and the provider-force prerequisite remain the hard core. Scripts committed under scripts/ghidra/. No game files changed.




### Session 28cc - coop_shim LAN short-circuit experiment: shim loads & hooks install, then CRASHES (scaffold stubs are non-functional). Rolled bin back.

Owner ran run_coop_shim_sandbox.bat (proxy+shim deployed). Evidence:
- shim LOADED + installed all 8 hooks (FC3 base 0x78660000, ASLR; patch addrs = base+RVA), forced online global, fired `online_flag -> 1`. It NEVER reached `shim: host -> ...` (co-op Host) - crashed during online-init.
- Windows Application Error: faulting `FC3_d3d11.dll + 0x01249226`, exception 0xc0000005 (two runs, pids 0x4870/0x1100). RVA 0x01249226 is just before/near FUN_112492a0 (RVA_rv_sandboxurl 0x012492a0) -> the crash is the caller of the rv_sandboxurl hook, right after it ran.

ROOT CAUSE (from coop_shim.cpp): the LAN hooks are STUBS. `hk_rv_sandboxurl` returns 1 but does NOT write the out sockaddr (`(void)out;` + TODO 28ad "write g_cfg.lan_ip into the exact struct layout the gate reads (FUN_11232e20/FUN_11232ef0)"). So the game consumes an uninitialized/garbage address struct and access-violates. Also `patch_5` stores saved bytes into a SHARED single `g_hooks[0].saved` slot (the documented install_hooks TODO), and several detours never call the original -> stack/return corruption risk. This is exactly what RUN.md warned: an evidence-gathering scaffold, not working co-op.

WHAT WORKS (good signal): the loader-proxy FAKE_ONLINE boot path + hook installation + online_flag short-circuit are solid; the RV auth short-circuit idea (host_handler) is sound. What is MISSING is real implementation of the address/port producers: hk_rv_sandboxurl and hk_gate must fill the exact sockaddr_in the gate consumes (need the FUN_11232e20 / FUN_11232ef0 output struct layouts), and patch_5 needs per-hook saved bytes / correct trampolines that preserve the ABI.

This LAN-short-circuit-via-shim is effectively Plan B implemented in coop_shim; finishing it = days of focused C + small RE (sockaddr struct layout) in coop_shim.cpp. Rolled bin back to the real loader + removed coop_shim.dll (normal play/mod-262 unaffected). ### Session 28cd - RE of the co-op gate address/port producers (FC3_d3d11.dll md5 46bcc097) -> exact sockaddr layout for the shim fix.

Decompiled FUN_11232e20, FUN_11232ef0, FUN_112492a0, FUN_11275d90, FUN_11237670 (project FC3d3d11_live). The gate reads a standard **sockaddr_in reached via a pointer stored at `this->[0]`** (the session/connection object):
- `FUN_11232e20` (address producer, thiscall): reads `*(u_long*)(*this + 4)` -> IPv4 at **sockaddr_in +4** in NETWORK byte order (via `inet_ntoa`); requires a readiness flag `*(byte*)(this + 0x48)` (param_1[0x12]) != 0 else it calls FUN_1122f870 (lazy init). Outputs the dotted string via FUN_11213990.
- `FUN_11232ef0` (port producer, fastcall): reads `*(u_short*)(*this + 2)` -> port at **sockaddr_in +2** in NETWORK byte order (ntohs). Same `this->[0]` sockaddr pointer + same `this+0x48` readiness flag.
=> the object layout to fake: `obj[0]` = pointer to `sockaddr_in{ sa_family(2)=AF_INET(2), sin_port(2)=htons(port), sin_addr(4)=inet_addr(ip), pad(8) }`, and `*(byte*)(obj+0x48)` set nonzero.

`FUN_112492a0` (the rv_sandboxurl net-op the shim hooks) is actually `void(void)`: it reads config key "SandboxUrl" (FUN_11213a10), calls the string parser FUN_11249220, then a gate FUN_11233110, and on success FUN_11235be0. The shim's `hk_rv_sandboxurl(void* out)` signature is WRONG (the real fn has no out param) and returning 1 without setting up the connection object leaves `obj[0]` sockaddr unset. The 28cc crash at RVA 0x01249226 is INSIDE the SandboxUrl string parser FUN_11249220 (called with an empty/invalid config value), NOT in the hook - so the stub does not stop the parser path.

REFINED FIX PLAN for coop_shim (much simpler than originally modeled): do NOT try to fill the rv_sandboxurl out-struct (there is none). Instead HOOK THE PRODUCERS directly:
1. Patch FUN_11232e20 (address) -> our handler returns/prints our fixed LAN IP string (skip reading obj sockaddr). Or set the global connection object's `[0]` to point at a static sockaddr_in we own.
2. Patch FUN_11232ef0 (port) -> our handler returns htons(LAN_PORT).
3. Ensure the `this+0x48` readiness path or bypass by hooking before it reads the sockaddr.
4. Prevent the SandboxUrl parser (FUN_11249220) from dereferencing a null/empty config: either supply a valid SandboxUrl string in fc3_config.json (e.g. "127.0.0.1:50000" style the parser accepts) so it parses cleanly, or short-circuit FUN_112492a0 earlier / provide the config.
This is a concrete, small shim change once the readiness flag + how to reach the connection `this` are pinned (next: find callers of FUN_11232e20/FUN_11232ef0 to get the `this` object, or hook them to ignore `this`). Artifacts: H:/FarCryCoop/scratch/ghidra/sockaddr_decompile.txt. No game files changed.

The AV (28cc fault RVA 0x01249226, parser entry +6) is the `*(this+4)` read or the first iterator deref when the config OBJECT is present-but-malformed/zeroed (length byte set, data pointer bad) - i.e. partially-initialized online state, not a simple empty string.

So the shim's own online_flag=1 (required to pass the co-op gate precondition) ACTIVATES a netconfig path that crashes offline. This is the same class as the 28ba/28bp A1 dead-end: the PC co-op path is welded to live online state; faking the flag wakes real online code that needs real server-populated data.

### Session 28cj - A2 AUTH FEASIBILITY RECON: FC3 co-op auth is the Quazal RendezVous "UbiAuthentication" service over PRUDP (NOT a bespoke Ubisoft crypto gate); public materials + both-endpoints-owned make A2 auth FEASIBLE (conditional green light).

DLL evidence (FC3_d3d11.dll md5 46bcc097 string scan, saved H:/FarCryCoop/scratch/fc3_auth_strings.txt): "gconnect"/"Agora" are NOT compiled strings (they are config hostnames). The auth stack is Quazal **RendezVous** (strings "Copyright ... Quazal Technologies ... Ubisoft", "Software\\Quazal", 2833x RendezVous, 19x PRUDP). Concrete auth classes/symbols: `JobUbiAuthenticationLogin::RVLogin`, `JobUbiAuthenticationLogin::PrepareLogin`, `UbiAuthenticationClient`, `UbiAuthenticationClientSSO`, `UbiAuthenticationLoginCustomDataDDL`/`UbiAuthenticationLoginCustomData`, `JobTicketManagerLogin::InitiateLogin`, `JobTicketManagerLoginWithData::InitiateLogin`, `JobTicketManagerAcquireTicket::{PrepareCall,ProcessResponse}`, `storm::echo::SessionTicket*`, `CFCXGatorLoginCheckOperation` ("Gator" is just EAccountFlags_Gator + a login-check op, not a separate service). So FC3 login = RV client posts a UbiAuthentication login (SSO custom-data blob) over PRUDP to the RV host (SandboxUrl), then TicketManager issues session tickets, then the GameSessionKey is derived for PRUDP payload crypto.

PUBLIC MATERIALS (web recon): (1) **PretendoNetwork/nex-go** - open-source PRUDP/NEX server library with an explicit "Quazal compatibility mode", PRUDPv0/v1 packets, fragmentation, reliable/unreliable, virtual ports, compression, RMC request/response messages, Kerberos. This is the transport layer, essentially off-the-shelf -> the 28bz "PRUDP LOW risk" is now near-solved. (2) A clean-room replacement of **Ghostbusters: The Video Game (PS3)** online: its shut-down **Quazal Rendez-Vous / NEX over PRUDP** back end reimplemented byte-for-byte purely from RE of the game's own traffic/binary -> proves a full RV back end is reconstructable end-to-end. (3) **"Reverse engineering of the Splinter Cell Blacklist protocol"** - SC Blacklist (2013) is the SAME Quazal-RV + UbiAuthentication family as FC3, the closest published protocol reference.

AUTH CRYPTO ANALYSIS: in production the RV UbiAuthentication service validates the Ubisoft-Connect SSO token against Ubisoft backend. For OUR emulated RV we CONTROL BOTH ENDS: the FC3 SDK account/SSO blob is faked by our loader proxy, and our RV server simply ACCEPTS whatever the client presents (no need to verify a real Ubisoft signature). The shim already observed the FC3 RV login sending dummy credentials ("UbiDummyPwd", FUN_11246b90), consistent with a client-side token the (now-missing) server trusted. So no Ubisoft private key is required on the server side.

RESIDUAL RISK (the one medium item): FC3's PRUDP per-connection key / Connection-Request signature constants (28bz/28ca: not found by static string xrefs). With nex-go as a concrete reference implementation, the reliable way to obtain them is a LIVE capture: once the FC3 client is forced to DIAL our RV host it emits a cleartext PRUDP Connection Request whose signature reveals the key scheme. Getting the client to dial without the full menu is the next technical puzzle (forcing the whole session-manager crashed the menu, 28ci; a more surgical force of just the login operation is the candidate).

FEASIBILITY VERDICT: A2 auth = FEASIBLE, conditional green light. Transport: low (nex-go). RV services (UbiAuthentication/SessionMaster/TicketManager): medium (public RV protocol + SC-Blacklist/Ghostbusters precedents, reconstructable by RE of FC3's RV client). Auth validation: low (we own both endpoints; server self-accepts). Residual: extract FC3 PRUDP key constants via a live Connection-Request capture. Still weeks, but the crypto wall that looked decisive in 28ba/28br is NOT a blocker.




### Session 28ck - RV login client RE'D: FC3 UbiAuthentication login uses DUMMY Quazal credentials read from config (DummyUbiUser@quazal.com / UbiDummyPwd + config key "h7fyctiuucf"), NOT a real Ubisoft SSO token. Major A2 de-risk.

Ghidra string-anchor (rv_login_client.txt): `JobUbiAuthenticationLogin::RVLogin` = FUN_11279d80 (__fastcall, the op Execute); `::PrepareLogin` = FUN_11279f90 (the op setup).
- PrepareLogin FUN_11279f90: `FUN_11213a10("h7fyctiuucf")` (config key -> value, some id/playlist), then reads config keys `"DummyUbiUser@quazal.com"` (-> user value) and `"UbiDummyPwd"` (-> password value), sets a flag uVar4=0x20 (EAccountFlags_Gator), calls FUN_112467c0(param_4,param_5,param_7,out) then FUN_11245f60(...) which packs (gameCtx, ?, customData, ?, PWD, USER, 0x20, ...) into the login request. Then registers the async op: sets *param_1 = &PTR_FUN_116cf86c (vtable), local_20 = FUN_11279d80 (the RVLogin Execute), name "JobUbiAuthenticationLogin::PrepareLogin", FUN_1121c8f0 enqueues the op.
- RVLogin FUN_11279d80: prechecks via FUN_11210b10 on config->rv fields; on failure returns RV precheck errors `0x80030064` / `0x80030065`; on ready path calls `FUN_11246b90(&local_5, param_1+0x58, local_10, &local_18, local_14, param_1[0x1e], param_1[0x68], 0)` = the RV UbiAuthentication LOGIN SEND (guest login with the dummy creds), then sets callback `FUN_11253890` ("JobUbiAuthenticationLogin::RVLogin" completion) and enqueues.

=> The FC3 retail co-op login authenticates to the RV UbiAuthentication service with a FIXED DUMMY account whose user/password come from the online CONFIG (which we serve). A2 FEASIBILITY NOW: auth validation risk = LOW (dummy creds, config-supplied, we own the config AND the server). Remaining = implement the Quazal RV/PRUDP protocol (login service + ticket/session + GameSessionKey) per nex-go + RV precedents, and capture FUN_11246b90's outgoing packet to learn the exact RV login message encoding. ### Session 28cl - login TRIGGER mapped: RV UbiAuthentication login fires ONLY from the co-op JOIN path, and is gated by rv_connected; the front-most A2 blocker is Ubisoft-Connect BOOT-AUTH whose code is NOT in the game bin.

PrepareLogin (FUN_11279f90) has exactly ONE caller: join_handler FUN_1127a1d0. That handler first calls precondition FUN_1127a0d0(param_2,...) and only if it returns nonzero does it build+run the JobUbiAuthenticationLogin (PrepareLogin). Per the shim notes FUN_1127a0d0 returns nonzero only when rv_connected is already true. So the RV login (dummy-creds UbiAuthentication) is reached only during a JOIN, and only after the RV client is ALREADY connected (rv_connected). The HOST path does not use JobUbiAuthenticationLogin (it uses SubAccountClient auth). => The FC3 client will not dial RV until FCX has brought up its online/RV subsystem (rv_connected), which happens during FCX online-init.

Where is the online-init gate / boot-auth? A string scan of every >50KB DLL in the game bin: gconnect / agora / Autologin appear in NONE of them. uplay_r1_loader.dll (294400) and upc_r1_loader.dll and ubiorbitapi_r2_loader.dll are loader STUBS with no auth strings. FC3.dll / FC3_d3d11.dll carry only the RV-side UbiAuthentication (the PRUDP co-op services, already analyzed), no gconnect/agora. Conclusion: gconnect/agora hostnames come from the ONLINE CONFIG and are consumed by the **Ubisoft Connect client libraries**, which live in the Ubisoft Connect install (NOT the game bin) and are loaded by the loader stub. So FCX's boot-time online-init (which creates the session-manager and sets rv_connected) waits on the **Ubisoft-Connect authentication handshake**, whose binary is outside the game folder.

REFINED A2 ROADMAP (risk moved to the front): the RV/PRUDP co-op services are feasible (dummy creds 28ck + nex-go transport + public RV protocol), BUT the client only dials RV after FCX online-init, which is gated behind Ubisoft-Connect boot-auth - the heaviest, least-FC3-specific step, and its code is not even in the game bin. So A2 milestone 1 is effectively defeating/emulating Ubisoft Connect (Uplay) boot-auth, not the RV server. NEXT INVESTIGATION: the public "online-fix" (emuparas) and similar Uplay/Ubisoft-Connect emulators already reimplement Ubisoft offline authentication for many Ubisoft titles - determine whether one exists/works for Far Cry 3; if so, boot-auth is solved off-the-shelf and A2 collapses back to the (feasible) RV-services task. Toolchain note: Go is NOT installed (nex-go would need it); Python/Node/git/MSVC are present, so the RV server could be Python/Node instead.




### Session 28cm - online-fix check: NO public online-fix/Uplay emulator covers Far Cry 3; the community confirms FC3 online/co-op is fully dead (only old-Uplay reaches the menu). Our proxy already reaches the co-op menu, so the sole hard nut is bringing up FCX online-init/rv_connected so the client dials our RV - unsolved by anyone.

Web recon (Steam FC3 + Splinter Cell Blacklist discussions): the exact "Far Cry 3 service is not available / служба недоступна" error is a known community dead-end - "you can't play any old game online on Ubisoft Connect" (Ubisoft killed legacy titles' servers). The only community workaround is installing an OLD Uplay build (Wayback Machine) in place of Ubisoft Connect with a version.txt no-update trick - and that only reaches the menu; the co-op/RV backend stays gone. There is no online-fix (emuparas) entry for FC3.

POSITIONING: our loader proxy FAKE_ONLINE ALREADY brings FC3 to the "Play Online Co-op" menu (28ch), i.e. we are at front-end parity with (or beyond) the community old-Uplay path without needing an old client. What no one has achieved (community or us) is getting past "service unavailable" to actually bring up FCX's online session-manager + rv_connected and make the client dial a (real or emulated) RendezVous host. That FCX-online-init / rv_connected bring-up is the unique hard nut of A2 and is gated against shallow fakes (28ci manager force crashed the menu; 28cl the RV login needs rv_connected).

VERDICT (consolidated, honest): A2 is feasible-in-principle at the RV/PRUDP layer (public protocol, dummy creds 28ck, nex-go/Python transport), but its critical path is an UNSOLVED FCX online-init/rv_connected bring-up that no existing tool achieves; it is genuine RE + implementation with real uncertainty, not a known-solved problem with a missing server. Decision point for owner stands: invest in cracking the FCX online-init bring-up (hard, uncertain) vs treat mod-262 solo offline co-op as the practical deliverable.




### Session 28cn - online-init mechanism RE'd: the manager is built by the game's OWN factory (FUN_10420a77 -> FUN_10418a3d factory vtable+4); principled next experiment = have the shim CALL that factory instead of writing a zeroed fake.

- Manager creator FUN_10420a77 (void): `piVar1=FUN_10418a3d(); DAT_11e42c90 = (**(code**)(*piVar1 + 4))();` - gets the online-manager FACTORY (lazy singleton FUN_10418a3d) and calls its vtable[+4] to CONSTRUCT the manager, storing into the global. Its sole caller is FUN_1041936b. So FCX builds the real manager via this factory; the 28ci zeroed stand-in crashed because it is not a constructed object.
- rv_connected getter FUN_112345d0 is NOT a bool: it is a RV connection state-machine op (object field param_1[3] = state: 1=connected; it runs FUN_112344e0/FUN_1122dd90 on states 2/3/4, sets state=1, reports Result 0x80010001 via Result.cpp, returns 1). So "connected" is a live storm::echo/RV connection object state, set only when the RV client actually connects.
- Join precondition FUN_1127a0d0 (thiscall on the join-op): bails unless (a) prior login op state field [8]==5 (done) and (b) FUN_11237670 (rv_connected) true; then builds the UbiAuthentication login via FUN_11279b40.

NEXT EXPERIMENT (principled replacement for the crashed FAKE_MGR): add coop_shim mode FC3COOP_FAKE_MGR=3 that, instead of writing a zeroed object into [0x11e42c90], CALLS the game's own creator FUN_10420a77 (or its caller FUN_1041936b) once the base module is present, letting FCX construct a REAL online manager. If the factory depends only on already-present online state, the real manager materializes and the co-op gate (FUN_10443663 !=0) passes without the 28ci zeroed-object crash. If the factory itself needs rv_connected/RV up, it will still fail - which tells us the online-init is truly gated behind a live RV connection and the whole chain (config -> RV connect -> rv_connected -> manager) must be satisfied by our emulated RV before ANY menu/co-op path works. Toolchain: implementable in the existing C shim (call via function pointer VA(RVA)+0). Artifacts: H:/FarCryCoop/scratch/ghidra/online_init.txt.




### Session 28co - Stage0c result: calling the game's creator FUN_10420a77 DOES build a REAL manager, but the game then crashes at a DETERMINISTIC site (0x449a65) because the manager's RV sub-object getter returns NULL - hard disasm proof that a live RV connection (not just a manager) is required.

run_a2_stage0c.bat (FC3COOP_FAKE_MGR=3, clean mode: no online flag, no hooks). coop_shim.log: "MGR_FORCER global after=0x4ded2850 (REAL manager built)" - the __try call to FUN_10420a77 returned WITHOUT exception and [0x11e42c90] became a real non-null manager (notably 'before' was already 0x07a3fa00, so the game had itself created a manager during FAKE_ONLINE boot too). Game advanced to "performing login" then CRASHED: FC3_d3d11.dll + 0x00449a65, 0xc0000005 - the SAME address as the 28ci zeroed-manager crash.

Disassembly at the fault (dis_fault.txt):
  10449a5a MOV ECX, [0x11e42c90]   ; ECX = online session-manager (now non-null)
  10449a60 CALL FUN_1041f965        ; ask the manager for a sub-object/ServiceMaster; returns EAX
  10449a65 MOV EDX, [EAX]  <-- AV   ; EAX == NULL -> null-deref
  10449a6e CALL [EDX+0x64]          ; would then dispatch a vtable method on that sub-object
So the manager's sub-object getter FUN_1041f965 returns NULL offline. This sub-object (an RV session/account ServiceMaster) exists only once the RV client connection is live. Conclusion (now with disasm evidence): a real manager is NOT sufficient; the manager's RV-backed sub-objects are null unless the RV connection is established (rv_connected). The client goes straight to USING the RV sub-object and null-derefs it - it does not first dial/connect RV in this path. Combined with 28ch (zero UDP ever sent), FCX never actually initiates the RV client connection in our FAKE_ONLINE flow, so every online sub-object stays null and every gate we pass just moves the null-deref to the next missing RV object.

NET A2 STATUS: the critical path is confirmed to be "establish the RV client connection (rv_connected) so the manager populates its RV sub-objects", and the client is NOT initiating that connection on its own in the fake-online boot. The next nut (hard, still open) is to make FCX actually START the RV NetEncoder/RendezVous connect toward our emulated host (SandboxUrl=127.0.0.1:50000). Until that connect happens, no emulated RV server has anything to answer and every manager-derived sub-object is null -> crash. Rolled bin back (game normal). This is the make-or-break unknown and is genuinely deep/uncertain.

An anonymous RV login (no UbiAuthentication user) may be a simpler path to bring the RV connection up than the dummy-user login.

ACTIONABLE: the make-or-break trigger is getting FCX to execute JobBackEndServicesLogin(WithData)::ConnectToAuthenticationService so JobConnectEndPoint dials SandboxUrl (127.0.0.1:50000) and ConnectionSucceeded populates manager+0x110. Next experiments: (a) hook/log FUN_11252f70 + FUN_11243d80 (TryDirectConnect) + FUN_1123d5b0 (ConnectionSucceeded) with a UDP listener on :50000 to see whether ANY connect is attempted and capture the first PRUDP Connection Request; (b) find callers of ConnectToAuthenticationService to locate the FCX login-exec entry that starts the whole chain (the online-init trigger we have not been able to fire). ### Session 28cq - the RV dial is NOT a simple callable entry: ConnectToAuthenticationService is a Quazal job-vtable method dispatched only when FCX's login state machine advances to LoggingIn, which requires Ubisoft Connect (outside the game folder) to have populated FCX online state. In-process forcing is structurally blocked; this is the wall.

Findings (rv_trigger.txt, gamestartlogin.txt):
- gameStartLogin string -> FUN_1086d307, but decompiling it shows it is a FRONT-END SCRIPT-COMMAND handler (builds/executes script command strings gameStart / postPvpLobby / postCoopLobby via FUN_100c7ce6, reads login status via FUN_107d8b6b etc). It is NOT the RV dial entry; calling it needs a command-args array (param_2 != 0) and it only issues menu script commands once login status is already ready.
- ConnectToAuthenticationService (FUN_11252f70 / FUN_11253020) is referenced only as DATA from FUN_112530d0 / FUN_112532a0 - i.e. as a slot in the login-Job vtable. It runs when the Quazal JobManager dequeues+executes JobBackEndServicesLogin. TryDirectConnect FUN_11243d80 is called only from FUN_11244310 (inside the JobConnectEndPoint job itself). So the whole dial chain is Quazal job plumbing enqueued by FCX online-init, not a directly-callable routine.
- The FCX login state machine (ELoginStatus_Invalid -> NotLoggedIn -> FetchOnlineConfig(Rdy) -> LoggingIn(Rdy) -> LoggingInFinalizing -> LoggedIn) is driven by the front-end; advancing past NotLoggedIn/FetchOnlineConfig into LoggingIn requires FCX to hold valid online credentials (UbiAuth passport ticket) which Ubisoft Connect fetches via gconnect/agora at boot. In our setup (FC3COOP_FAKE_ONLINE boots the game but Ubisoft Connect never authenticates), FCX LoginStatus never reaches LoggingIn, so no back-end login job is ever enqueued, JobConnectEndPoint never dials SandboxUrl, ConnectionSucceeded never runs, manager+0x110 (ServiceMaster) stays null -> the 28co null-deref.

CONCLUSION (structural, now fully traced end to end): every online/co-op bring-up gate in FC3 in-process resolves to "FCX login state machine must reach LoggingIn", and that advance is gated on Ubisoft Connect boot-auth having populated FCX's online credentials. That code is in the Ubisoft Connect install (gconnect/agora strings are NOT in any game-folder DLL, NOTES 28cl), and FC3 online services are dead for everyone (28cm). Forcing it in-process only relocates the null-deref to the next unpopulated RV object (28cg netconfig, 28ci menu, 28co manager+0x110). The in-process route to multi-human co-op is therefore blocked at a structural boundary that is not inside our binary.

DECISION POINT FOR OWNER: A2 (local RV emulator) needs the client to voluntarily dial our host, and the client only dials when FCX login reaches LoggingIn - which needs real Ubisoft Connect online (dead). Continuing means reverse-engineering Ubisoft Connect's boot-auth + FCX credential injection, a much larger and externally-gated effort. Recommended practical deliverable remains mod-262 solo offline co-op (working), with all RV findings documented for a future attempt. Rolled bin back (game normal).

### Session 28cs - Route A3a is architecturally confirmed and the API contract is now known: the game imports the ENTIRE co-op-via-friends surface from uplay_r1_loader.dll (which is OUR proxy), so the proxy can implement party + game-session + events itself and bridge two local games, no uplay_r14.dll or RV needed.

Import-graph facts (probe_uplay_exports.py, local/scratch/uplay_exports.txt):
- uplay_r14.dll is 32-bit x86 (machine 0x14c), ImageBase 0x10000000, 136 exports - the real Ubisoft Uplay client.
- FC3_d3d11.dll (the game) does NOT import uplay_r14.dll directly. It imports `uplay_r1_loader.dll` and `ubiorbitapi_r2_loader.dll`. uplay_r1_loader.dll (the DLL we already ship as a proxy) forwards to uplay_r14.dll. ubiorbitapi_r2_loader.dll only does savegames + StartLauncher (orbit client), not multiplayer.
- The exact UPLAY functions the game imports from uplay_r1_loader.dll (this is the FULL contract the proxy must satisfy for the friends/party/session flow):
  lifecycle: UPLAY_Init, UPLAY_Update, UPLAY_Quit, UPLAY_Release, UPLAY_GetLastError, UPLAY_GetNextEvent, UPLAY_HasOverlappedOperationCompleted, UPLAY_GetOverlappedOperationResult
  user: UPLAY_USER_GetCredentials, UPLAY_USER_GetCdKeys, UPLAY_USER_IsConnected
  friends: UPLAY_FRIENDS_Init, UPLAY_FRIENDS_GetFriendList
  party: UPLAY_PARTY_Init, UPLAY_PARTY_IsPartyLeader, UPLAY_PARTY_SetGuest, UPLAY_PARTY_SetUserData, UPLAY_PARTY_GetFullMemberList, UPLAY_PARTY_InvitePartyToGame
  session: UPLAY_SetGameSession, UPLAY_ClearGameSession
  misc: UPLAY_OVERLAY_Show, UPLAY_AVATAR_GetAvatarIdForCurrentUser

DESIGN (A3a): make our uplay_r1_loader proxy answer the party/session/event calls with a LOCAL shared registry so two co-located games act as online friends in one party with an open game session:
1. Shared cross-process state (Windows named shared-memory section, e.g. Global\FC3COOP_Party, or a tiny local UDP/TCP rendezvous the proxy runs) holding: the party (members: {account, name, leader}), each member's GameSession (the opaque blob the game passed to SetGameSession + Joinability), and a pending-event queue.
2. Host game: calls SetGameSession(data,Open) -> proxy stores host's session blob + marks joinable; returns success. Host is IsPartyLeader=true.
3. Client game: proxy returns a friend list (GetFriendList) containing the host, and GetFullMemberList returns the party with the host; IsPartyLeader=false. Client's UI shows the host's joinable co-op game.
4. Join: driven by a UPLAY event delivered through UPLAY_GetNextEvent (friend party update / game invite accepted). The proxy must emit the correct UPLAY_Event_t type + payloads so the game's handler proceeds to dial the HOST'S OWN game data (the co-op data blob, which uses the engine's own LAN/loopback storm transport once the session is 'joined'), NOT Ubisoft.
5. Must keep UPLAY_USER_IsConnected=true and IsInOfflineMode=false so the front-end shows the online co-op UI (the 28h-era gate).

OPEN QUESTION (next RE): exact struct layouts the game consumes - UPLAY_Event_t + event type enum (what GetNextEvent fills), UPLAY_Friend_t (GetFriendList), UPLAY_PartyMember_t (GetFullMemberList), and the SetGameSession blob format. The real uplay_r14.dll implements these correctly, so disassemble in uplay_r14.dll: UPLAY_GetNextEvent (RVA 0x115ea), UPLAY_PARTY_GetFullMemberList (0xe0fc), UPLAY_FRIENDS_GetFriendList (0x178be), UPLAY_SetGameSession (0x3ce2), UPLAY_PARTY_Init (0x6eba). The Uplay SDK v1.x public headers likely define the structs - cross-check.

Toolchain fits: our existing uplay_proxy already exports the UPLAY entry points the game imports (it currently forwards to real uplay_r14); extend those handlers with the local party/session registry + event pump. No RV/PRUDP, no C: reads required.
