### Session 28g9 - static RC4 probe (whole-binary heuristic scan) did NOT isolate the OpenSSL RC4; it did map the OTHER crypto; the exact RV cipher stays reachable only via the connection socket-driver vtable

Owner asked for a no-run static probe to find the RC4 KSA/PRGA in FC3_d3d11.dll and decrypt rv.pcap offline. Two full-binary Ghidra scans (scripts_live/FindRC4.java -> rc4_cands.txt, FindRC4b.java -> rc4b.txt; scratch, untracked): pass1 = instruction prefilter (AND-imm-0xff>=2 & XOR & MOVZX/ADD) over 177,723 functions -> 47 candidates, 27 with SHR-0x10; pass2 = big stack frame (S[256]-sized 0x100..0x400) + XOR + 2-register indexed byte loads -> 1390 big-frame, 170 candidates. RESULT: the fingerprints isolated CRC32 (FUN_10041ef0 / 1115e340 / 11267070 / 10e3f8f0: `acc >> 8 ^ uint_table[(acc&0xff ^ byte)]`), AES/Rijndael (FUN_112ea9a0, 290 XOR, four 1KB T-tables at DAT_116d7d78/116d8178/116d8578/116d8978/116d8d78; FUN_11429b00..1142e71b AES keyschedule with `*8` T-tables), a parallel-bits POPCOUNT (FUN_113eae30 - the `l + (l>>0x10)` "OpenSSL RC4 KSA" look-alike is actually the `0x55555555/0x33333333/0x0f0f0f0f/0xf000f` Hamming-weight fold), a network login handshake (FUN_10f60140), and color/YUV converts (FUN_111e73d0 / 111e8940). The REAL RV stream cipher (RC4) was NOT isolated: OpenSSL 0.9.8 RC4 with byte-typed x/y/state emits NO explicit `AND 0xff` immediates (byte assignment truncates), so it slipped both prefilters, and the distinctive `l + (l>>16)` I hoped was RC4-specific collided with popcount. Lesson for a future attempt: do NOT pattern-match RC4 in the C; find it STRUCTURALLY - locate the write to the connection object's socket-driver pointer (conn+0x9c, the 28g3 slot that read 0x0 live), read the STATIC vtable it is set to (in the connection constructor), dump that vtable, and decompile the send slot: that callee is the RC4. mod-262 SOLO unchanged.




### Session 28g10 - third RC4 probe (KSA 256-loop-bound signal) also failed to isolate a compact RC4 -> static/heuristic hunt CLOSED; the cipher needs the runtime driver vtable (blocked) or is not stock RC4

Follow-up no-run scan FindRC4c.java (rc4c.txt): prefilter = KSA loop-bound `CMP reg,0x100` (256-byte S init, a signal byte-typed RC4 cannot drop) + XOR>=2 + >=4 byte-indexed loads, over all 177,723 functions -> 152 candidates, all of them LARGE game functions (4.4K-62K decompiled chars, byte-shuffle in asset/serializer code), none a compact self-contained RC4 KSA/PRGA. Three whole-binary probes (28g9 two + this one) converge on the same fact: the RV stream cipher is not isolable from the decompiled C by structure or constants; either OpenSSL RC4 with byte-typed state hides from every cheap fingerprint, or Quazal prudps uses its own obfuscator, not stock RC4. The only reliable routes left both need the runtime connection socket-driver vtable (conn+0x9c) whose live read was 0x0 (blocked), or a far deeper caller-chain RE with no remaining cheap entry. STATIC/heuristic RC4 hunt is CLOSED. Standing decision unchanged: online RV-emulation frontier parked; mod-262 SOLO offline co-op is the shipped, owner-confirmed deliverable.

### Session 28i2 - live hook rebuilt: capture BOTH RV send wrappers (the 28g8 run sent via sendto, not WSASendTo), ABI-safe sendto PRE capture, scan the real driver offset, and pair shim-PRE vs pcap-ciphertext offline

Owner chose the one concrete live experiment: stop the RV-ENC/RV-PROBE collision, read the socket-driver pointer correctly (it read 0x0 at conn+0x9c in 28g3), capture a plaintext/ciphertext pair, recover the prudps keystream, decrypt rv.pcap. Findings that shaped the fix: (a) the wsasendto collision was ALREADY handled (install_rv_probe skips RV-PROBE on 0x01214630 when FC3COOP_RV_ENC=1); the reason RV-ENC saw 0 in the 28g8 run is that the RV data left via the OTHER wrapper FUN_11218510 (sendto, RV-PROBE logged 2) while RV-ENC only hooked FUN_11214630 (WSASendTo). (b) The comment confirmed in SigSend.java: at FUN_11214630, when the secured socket driver *(this+0x9c) is present the function does NOT raw-send, it calls the driver vtable send (slot +0x10 addr / +0x1c plain) which applies the prudps cipher, so param_2 is the PLAIN packet - but we never observed *(this+0x9c)!=0, so the driver field is not at +0x9c for the connections we hit.
coop_shim changes (build clean, coop_shim.dll 155,648 B): (1) new RV-ENC-ST capture on the sendto wrapper FUN_11218510 as an ABI-NEUTRAL observer (pushfd/pushad, PRE dumper reads buf=sp[1]/len=sp[2], popad/popfd, jmp trampoline) so it cannot corrupt the stack regardless of arg count (POST/ciphertext comes from the pcap); install_rv_probe now also skips the RV-PROBE sendto observer when RV_ENC is on (new same-address guard). (2) rv_this_window_scan(thisp): dumps the thisp window +0x80..0x110 and scans +0x0..0x140 for any in-module field that behaves like a socket-driver object (an object whose in-module vtable has in-module fn ptrs at +0x10/+0x1c); logs every candidate so the REAL driver offset is found from the log (log-only, no writes). Called from both RV-ENC and RV-ENC-ST. (3) rv_enc_extract.py: HDR/POSTH now parse the ENC-ST tag; new pair_with_pcap() pairs a shim PRE dump (plaintext) with the matching pcap ciphertext by (connId, len, seq byte 8) requiring cleartext header b0..b4 to agree, giving keystream = PRE^CT over the body (skips PREs that are already ciphertext because their xor is ~zero); main wires it into --apply and merges with in-place keystreams. stop_d0_reallogin.ps1 now counts RV-ENC-ST + THIS driver-candidate hits and auto-runs the extractor against the newest scripts/rv_capture/rv*.pcap. run_d0_reallogin.ps1 now tells the owner to run pktmon_rv_capture.ps1 150 in a second elevated window so the pairing has a same-session pcap. Next: one owner run -> read RV-ENC-ST PRE (plaintext) + THIS[] window (real driver offset), pair with the fresh pcap; if a real driver offset is found, the NEXT run hooks its send slot for a direct in-place keystream. mod-262 SOLO unchanged.


### Session 28i3 - the 28i2 run diagnosed WHY the send wrappers never fired: FUN_112161b0 takes the secured-driver branch (skips FUN_11214630); moved the driver scan+swap into RV-PKT (the path that actually fires)
Owner ran the 28i2 harness. Result: RV reached (RV-PROBE ConnectToAuthenticationService x3; RV-PKT on FUN_112161b0 fired 67x, connId d9deda3a) but RV-ENC=0 and RV-ENC-ST=0 -> NEITHER send wrapper (WSASendTo FUN_11214630 nor sendto FUN_11218510) was called. Diagnosis: the 28g4 run that saw RV-ENC=77x hit the RAW-WSASendTo branch (driver=0); THIS run hit the SECURED-DRIVER branch, where FUN_112161b0 sends through the driver VTABLE and never calls FUN_11214630 - so the wrapper hooks are bypassed, and THIS-scan/RV-DRV never armed because they were only invoked from rv_enc_capture. The RV-PKT entry buffer is still ciphertext (the object carries already-encrypted bytes), so RV-PKT PRE is not the plaintext.
FIX (coop_shim.dll 156,160 B, build clean): (1) rv_drv_hook_resolve now SCANS thisp+0x0..0x200 for the field whose object's vtable has in-module fn ptrs at BOTH +0x10 and +0x1c (the secured-driver send pair) instead of reading the hard-coded +0x9c (which is 0), logs the found offset, and swaps those vtable slots. (2) rv_pkt_capture (FUN_112161b0 - the path that actually fires 67x) now, before invoking the original, calls rv_this_window_scan(thisp,"pkt-conn") (idx<=3, logs the real driver-field offset) and rv_drv_hook_resolve(thisp) (arms the driver send-slot swap), so THIS very call's driver send should be captured in-place by RV-DRV10/RV-DRV1C => payload_changed=1 => PRE^POST IS the prudps keystream. Residual risk: RV-DRV repl stubs assume the driver send method cleans 4/3 stack args (ret16/ret12); if the class is wrong the game crashes - but the RV-DRV 'found driver at thisp+0x..' log line then pins the real vtable for a precise next hook. One more owner run (same 2-window recipe) is the test.
